Thus the table has 25 PASS entries, no FAIL entry, and one explicitly
unproved target interface. The latter does not invalidate the finite-word
lemma. No suggested correction to the frozen object is necessary on the
basis of this audit.

## 1. Definitions, dependencies, and what is being checked

All vectors and norms are real Euclidean. A word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))
has finite length n>=0 and finite nonnegative entries. Physical GD is
x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L) grad f(x_t), where L>0. The normalized worst-case
value W_n(h) is the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)

over finite dimensions, convex differentiable functions with L-Lipschitz
gradient and an attained minimum, minimizers x_star, and x_0!=x_star.
The project convention is W=2R, where R is the same supremum without
the leading factor2. A choice of the closest minimizer gives
a legal instance of this supremum; it must be proved closest if that
distance is asserted to be exact.

The original benchmark is U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, where C_0=B_0=1 and

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).

These definitions match the statement of accepted fact04219554e41d9750.
No ray estimate, restricted split recurrence, computed benchmark value,
or conditional seed conclusion from that fact is used here.

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, whose full statement and proof were read,
fixes the ordered residual convention

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The audit derives Q_ij<=0 directly for the constructed functions. It does
not invoke that fact to delete incoming terminal rows or any prefix row.
The source paper is used to audit attribution and normalization. No
external lemma is an unchecked construction premise. All new reasoning
below is part of this single unverified audit, not a collection of accepted
intermediate facts.

For readability only, in the rest of this report c,d,T,H,A denote the
frozen draft's c_sp,d_sp,T_sp,H_sp,A_sp. Theta, D_i, ell_i, e_t and other
local letters retain the frozen draft's explicitly stated meanings in
this report; they do not redefine conflicting entries in the project
glossary. Coordinates of the global space are indexed0,...,k.

## 2. Primary-source normalization and applicability

The exact source is Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is (Almost)
Optimal for Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152v2, September10,2026,
[primary HTML](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2).
Its Eq(1) divides by (L/2)||x_0-x_star||^2: its performance symbol equals
project W. Lemma2.2 states the weaker prefactor1/[4(1+s_tail)]. Section3.1
and AppendixA.3 Eq(40) construct F(x_n)=D^2/[4(1+s_tail)] with initial
point e_1 and minimizer0, and explicitly discard the extra factor2 from
Eq(1). The stronger prefactor1/[2(1+s_tail)] therefore belongs to the
construction, not the literal lemma statement.

AppendixA.1 gives the frozen H_Y; AppendixA.2 Eq(35) gives
C_Y=epsilon+(H_Y+1)/epsilon. Section3.1 and AppendixA.3 require local
steps h/2, local checkpoint b/2, local A=c_Y a/2, hence physical
b>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)) and a>=2C_Y/c_Y. Write B_0^Y=2(1+epsilon^(-2))
for this source threshold, distinct from the benchmark B_0=1.
Section3.2 additionally imposes a>=max{2,2B_0^Y+2}. These are the exact
hypotheses being compared.

The exact downloaded HTML and extracted mathematical text are preserved
under this audit directory's `downloads/2609.09152v2.{html,txt}`.
The definition, relevant statements, full AppendixA.1--A.3 proofs, and
the extra recursion floors were read. Matlas retrieval returned unrelated
small-step self-contraction and convex-hull results; none was imported.

## 3. Parameters and all supporting normals

Take exactly 0<kappa<=epsilon<=1/2 and define

    Theta=arctan(1/epsilon),
    c=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa Theta), d=1-kappa^2,
    T=(exp(d Theta/kappa)-1)/d,
    H=c[1+(1+kappa)T]-1, A=epsilon+(H+1)/epsilon.

Here 0<Theta<pi/2, c>1, d>=3/4, T>0, H>0, A>0, and all are finite.
The curve and its proposed normal are

    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    e_t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    R_sp(theta)=c exp(-kappa theta),
    v(theta)=R_sp(theta)e(theta),
    n_sp(theta)=e(theta)+kappa e_t(theta), 0<=theta<=Theta.

Writing v=(p,-q), the endpoint identity follows from
cos Theta=epsilon/sqrt(1+epsilon^2) and
sin Theta=1/sqrt(1+epsilon^2): v(0)=(c,0), v(Theta)=(epsilon,-1).
The derivatives are

    p'=-R_sp(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,
    q'= R_sp(cos theta-kappa sin theta)>=0.

The second sign follows from cot theta>=cot Theta=epsilon>=kappa,
with theta=0 handled directly. Thus epsilon<=p<=c, 0<=q<=1, and
q/p=tan theta<=1/epsilon. These signs are proved, not assumed for an
arbitrary curve outside this parameter range.

Let K be the convex hull of0 and the compact curve. For any two angles
theta,phi in the full interval, put Delta=phi-theta. Then

    <n_sp(theta),v(phi)>/R_sp(theta)
       = exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+kappa sin Delta).

The derivative with respect to Delta is
-(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta. It is nonnegative for Delta<=0
and nonpositive for Delta>=0 on [-Theta,Theta]; the maximum is1 at0.
Also <n_sp(theta),0>=0<R_sp(theta). Consequently

    <n_sp(theta),w-v(theta)><=0 for EVERY w in K.

This includes both endpoints, all nonadjacent curve points, and every
convex combination. The additional normal (0,1) at v(0) is valid because
K is in the closed lower half-plane. No local curvature test is being
substituted for global supporting inequalities.

## 4. Exact backward construction, including the cap

For any ordinary block alpha_1,...,alpha_m>=0 start with
r_m=v_m=v(Theta). Write r_j=(u_j,-y_j). Assume the frozen induction
invariants u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon. At a noncapped
state also write

    r_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e(theta_j)+kappa t_j e_t(theta_j)],
    t_j>=0.

This representation holds initially with t_m=0. For alpha=alpha_j,
the capped test is y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)c]. If it holds, set
mu=u_j+(alpha-1)c. Then mu>=y_j/kappa>0 and

    r_j+alpha v(0)-v(0)
       =mu n_sp(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j).

Both coefficients of the valid normals are nonnegative. Their sum
proves that v(0) is the projection of r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(0) on K.
The new point obeys y_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-c). At any earlier step
alpha'>=0 this implies y_(j-1)<=kappa[u_(j-1)+(alpha'-1)c]. Hence
capping persists. There is no assumption that a huge ordinary step
must remain on the curved part.

If the cap test fails, a previously capped state is impossible. Define

    F_j(theta)=<r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)e_t(theta)>/R_sp(theta).

The current representation gives F_j(theta_j)=1, while failure of
the cap test gives F_j(0)=(u_j-y_j/kappa)/c<1-alpha. Direct differentiation
gives

    F_j'(theta)=(kappa+kappa^(-1))
                  (u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R_sp(theta)>0.

Since 1-alpha<=1, there is one and only one root
theta in(0,theta_j] with F_j(theta)=1-alpha. Moreover, if
Delta=theta_j-theta, then

    <r_j,e_t(theta)>
       =R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)sin Delta+kappa t_j cos Delta]>=0.

The root equation therefore says that
r_j+(alpha-1)v(theta) is a nonnegative multiple of n_sp(theta).
It proves the projection of r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(theta), and gives its
new representation with t_(j-1)>=0. This is an exact scalar definition,
not a solver invocation or a feasibility guess.

Finally the actual additions are
u_(j-1)=u_j+alpha p(theta), y_(j-1)=y_j+alpha q(theta).
Their signs preserve u>=epsilon and y>=1. The new ratio y/u is the
weighted average of y_j/u_j and q/p with nonnegative weights summing
to1, so it remains <=1/epsilon. This closes the induction. For alpha=0,
the noncapped root is theta_j and the entire state is unchanged; a
capped state remains unchanged too. No ordering assumption on the
magnitudes of alpha_j is used.

## 5. Uniform displacement bounds

Define P=sum_j alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),
Q=sum_j alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), and S=sum_j alpha_j. Then
r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), 0<=P<=cS, Q>=0, and P>=epsilon Q by the
pointwise curve bounds.

For two consecutive noncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new, let
Delta=theta_new-theta_old. Tangential components in the exact update
give

    t_old=B(Delta)+A_turn(Delta)t_new,
    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa,
    A_turn(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. With d=1-kappa^2,

    A_turn'-(d/kappa)A_turn
       =-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0,

so A_turn<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Also B<=Delta/kappa gives
1+dB<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Thus

    1+d t_old<=exp(d Delta/kappa)(1+d t_new).

The angle increments telescope to at most Theta-theta_j, irrespective
of the number of steps or their sizes. Starting at t_m=0 gives
t_j<=(exp(d(Theta-theta_j)/kappa)-1)/d<=T.
At every noncapped point,

    y_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)sin theta_j+kappa t_j cos theta_j]
       <=c[1+(1+kappa)T]=1+H.

A capped update has q=0 and leaves y unchanged. Hence this bound
extends to every state and Q=y_0-1<=H. All limiting or long-word claims
used here are actual finite inequalities with constants independent
of the word.

## 6. Finite local functions and the required proximal bounds

For a selected local checkpoint b>=1+epsilon^(-2), set

    A_loc=epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon,
    M=P+max{epsilon b,A_loc}, sigma=1/M,
    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(epsilon+P,-1-Q),
    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b), Y=sigma Q.

M>0, sigma>0, U>=0, Y>=0. Let V_fin consist of0, v(0), and all
v_j of the block, and let

    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>.

Every sampled v_j belongs to conv(V_fin), which is contained in K.
The global normal inequalities in Section3 therefore show that v_j
is still the projection of r_j onto this finite hull. At

    x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j, z_j=x_j-sigma v_j,

the plane with slope sigma v_j is active: its value is at least
every other plane value by the projection inequality. Thus
sigma v_j is a subgradient of phi at z_j, proving the proximal
relation and the local gradient of env_1 phi at x_j.

For any nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin,

    <v,-z_circ>
      =-p+sigma[p(epsilon+P)+q(1+Q)]
      <=-p+sigma p(P+A_loc)<=0.

Since p,q>=0, all its plane values remain nonpositive if the first
coordinate decreases from0 and the second increases from0. Including
the zero plane proves phi=0 on {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}.
The other zero region follows from the exact identity

    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0.

Indeed q<=p/epsilon and b>=1+epsilon^(-2). Equality at the threshold
is allowed. Increasing y above Y only reduces the expression, so
phi(U,y)=0 for every y>=Y.

The ordinary path starts at(1,0), ends at
(U+sigma epsilon b,Y), and has gradient sigma(epsilon,-1) there.
The physical checkpoint b gives (U,Y+sigma b).
For globalizing a maximum, positions alone are insufficient: we need
the proximal coordinates. They satisfy

    (z_j)_1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0,
    (z_j)_2=sigma[1+Q-y_j+q_j] in[0,sigma(1+Q)].

For a noncapped state,
u_j-p_j=R_sp(theta_j)t_j(cos theta_j-kappa sin theta_j)>=0.
For a capped state its normal representation gives u_j>=c=p_j.
Also 1<=y_j<=1+Q and0<=q_j<=1, proving the second line. These
bounds include the checkpoint query itself. They are the buffer used
by the max-envelope construction, and explain its b-1 transfer.

## 7. The one static max-envelope function

Partition the prescribed physical word, without reordering, into
(block_0,b_0,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k); let s_i be the ordinary
block sums. For i<k use the preceding construction on the actual steps
of block_i. Define

    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),
    ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i),
    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i),
    delta_tail=D_k/(1+2s_k),
    Phi_k(z)=delta_tail max{z_k-ell_k,0},
    J(z)=max_(0<=i<=k) Phi_i(z),
    f(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].

All D_i and delta_tail are positive, since b_i-1>=epsilon^(-2)>0.
All ell_i are nonnegative. This fixes one finite maximum of affine
planes before the run; it does not splice functions after queries.
Each Phi_i is nonnegative and vanishes at0 by its pre-activation zero
region; the hinge does too. Hence J>=0 and J(0)=0.

Because J is finite, continuous, convex and nonnegative, the quadratic
in its envelope is coercive and strictly convex. It has a unique
minimizer z at each x. The minimizing equation is g=x-z in partial J(z).
For x',z',g' the two subgradient inequalities imply

    ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>.

For an arbitrary increment w, comparison with the trial proximal point z
gives the upper bound
f(x+w)<=f(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. Subgradient support at z, followed by
expansion at the actual proximal point z' for x+w, gives

    f(x+w)>=f(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.

These inequalities imply differentiability with gradient g and a
global supporting hyperplane at each x, hence convexity. The displayed
monotonicity inequality and Cauchy--Schwarz give1-Lipschitz gradient.
The minimum is attained and is0 because f>=0 and f(0)=0.

## 8. Global trajectory, terminal, and every ordered row

At the start of block_i the proposed global position has frozen
coordinates ell_j+D_j U_j for j<i, current coordinate ell_i+D_i,
and all later coordinates0. This is e_0 when i=0.
Use the local path in the current pair of coordinates, with the
other coordinates fixed. At every candidate global proximal point:

* The current Phi_i has the prescribed subgradient and nonnegative value.
* If i>=1, Phi_(i-1) has first input U_(i-1). The current proximal
  coordinate is at least ell_i+D_i U_i>=ell_i, so its second input is
  at least ell_i/D_(i-1)=sigma_(i-1)(1+Q_(i-1))>=Y_(i-1).
* For j<=i-2, the first input of Phi_j remains U_j. Its frozen second
  coordinate is ell_(j+1)+D_(j+1)U_(j+1)>=ell_(j+1)>=D_j Y_j.
* If i+1<k, Phi_(i+1) has first input at most0 because the current
  second proximal coordinate is <=ell_(i+1); its other input is0.
* Every more distant future pair has first input -ell_j/D_j<=0 and
  second input0. The terminal hinge satisfies the same inactivity
  comparison when it is a future component.

Thus every component except possibly the current one is0 at that
proximal point. The current component dominates and its subgradient
is a subgradient of J, also when several components tie at0. This
proves the actual global gradient at every ordinary and checkpoint
query. No old or future plane has been removed.
After the checkpoint the active first coordinate freezes at
ell_i+D_i U_i; the next coordinate is

    D_i(Y_i+sigma_i b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_i sigma_i(b_i-1)
                       =ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).

This closes induction using exactly the original chronological word.

In the terminal block, let u be the sum of already applied tail steps,
so0<=u<=s_k. The last position is ell_k+D_k-u delta_tail. Its proposed
proximal position is at least ell_k because

    D_k-(u+1)delta_tail
       >=D_k-(s_k+1)D_k/(1+2s_k)>=0.

The earlier components are in their zero regions there. Thus the gradient
is delta_tail e_k at every tail query and at the unqueried terminal.
At equality in the proximal comparison, delta_tail is a permitted
subgradient of the hinge. Evaluation gives exactly

    f(x_n)=delta_tail D_k-(s_k+1/2)delta_tail^2
          =D_k^2/[2(1+2s_k)].

For any two samples i,j of this actual function, including the terminal,
write z_i=x_i-g_i. Since g_i is a subgradient of J at z_i and
f_i=J(z_i)+||g_i||^2/2, expansion with the convention of
fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_ij=J(z_i)-J(z_j)+<g_i,z_j-z_i><=0.

This is one identity for EVERY ordered pair. A minimizer is also a
valid sample, with g_star=0 and J(z_star)=0. In particular both
directions of every optimum, prefix, remote, and terminal row hold.
Zero steps give repeated actual positions and gradients but are not
deleted. The full Gram matrix is formed from the displayed actual
vectors in R^(k+1), so is positive semidefinite without a support or
rank assumption on formal data.

## 9. The exact closest minimizer and payoffs(4),(5)

For k>=1 define

    x_star=(1-sigma_0(epsilon+P_0), sigma_0(1+Q_0),0,...,0).

The first local pair is exactly at its center z_circ, so all its
planes have value0. The next first input is0 because its coordinate
equals ell_1; any later first input is nonpositive and its second
input0. The terminal hinge also vanishes. Therefore J(x_star)=0.
Since both terms in the envelope are nonnegative and the minimum is
attained, argmin f={J=0}: zero envelope value forces z=x and J(x)=0.

Every first-component slope is active at x_star. The exact identity

    e_0-x_star
      =[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/c] sigma_0(c,0,0,...)
        +(1+Q_0)sigma_0(epsilon,-1,0,...)

has nonnegative coefficients because P_0>=epsilon Q_0. If z belongs
to {J=0}, each active first-component plane gives a nonpositive inner
product of its slope with z-x_star. Thus
<e_0-x_star,z-x_star><=0 for every minimizer z. Expanding distance,

    ||e_0-z||^2=||e_0-x_star||^2+||z-x_star||^2
                 -2<e_0-x_star,z-x_star>
              >=||e_0-x_star||^2.

This proves that the displayed x_star is the closest minimizer of this
same function, uniquely as a projection, and that its squared radius is

    sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2]>0.

Combining with the terminal value and D_k recursion cancels exactly
sigma_0^2 and yields the frozen true-distance bound

    W_n(h)>=(b_0-1)^2/
       ([(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k))
       * product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i]^2.                 (A4)

For the mass-only bound, epsilon<=1 and nonnegativity imply

    sqrt((epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2)
       <=P_0+epsilon+(1+Q_0)/epsilon<=M_0,
    M_i<=c s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A}.

These are in the correct directions for a lower bound on the payoff.
They prove

    W_n(h)>=1/(1+2s_k)
       * product_(i=0)^(k-1)
           [(b_i-1)/(c s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A})]^2.           (A5)

The numerator is b_i-1 throughout. For k=0 the separate one-dimensional
function env_1(delta max{z,0}), delta=1/(1+2s_0), starts at1, has
closest minimizer0, keeps gradient delta through the full tail, and has
2f(x_n)=1/(1+2s_0). Multiplying any of these functions by L gives the
required physical h_t/L update and the same normalized payoff.

## 10. The distinct source-format realization

Here the parameters and curve are unchanged, but the function and
activation levels are intentionally different. Use any a_sp>=2A/c.
In each local block apply the preceding construction to alpha=h/2,
with local sum S=s_i/2, local checkpoint b=b_i/2, local allowance
A_half=c a_sp/2>=A, and

    sigma=1/(c S+epsilon b+A_half).

The physical condition b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)) gives the required
local threshold. Since P<=cS and A_loc<=A,
sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1 and sigma(P+A_loc)<=1. These are exactly the
two scale inequalities used for the zero regions. Thus the local
envelope f_i=env_1 phi_i has all previously proved local properties,
with corresponding U_i,Y_i. Its unbuffered local position transfer is

    G_i=sigma b=b_i/[c(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i].

Define new numbers D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,
D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i, ell'_(i+1)=D'_i Y_i, and

    F(x)=(1/2) sum_(i<k) (D'_i)^2
              f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)
          +(1/2) H_delta(x_k-ell'_k),
    delta=D'_k/(1+s_k),
    H_delta(z)=0 if z<=0;
               z^2/2 if0<=z<=delta;
               delta z-delta^2/2 if z>=delta.

Each rescaled pair term is convex and1-smooth on its two coordinates.
The hinge envelope H_delta is convex and1-smooth in one coordinate.
Every coordinate occurs in at most two terms. Summing their quadratic
upper bounds with weight1/2 gives remainder at most ||w||^2/2.
To explicitly infer gradient Lipschitzness, apply this upper bound
to F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x, with increment -(grad F(x)-grad F(y)).
Convexity makes y a global minimizer of the tilted function, giving

    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>
       >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.

The inequality with x,y exchanged gives the gradient contraction after
addition and Cauchy--Schwarz. It also proves all ordered interpolation
rows for F, including its terminal and the minimizer0.

Every term is nonnegative and vanishes at0, so min F=F(0)=0. Along
the actual local positions the current first coordinate remains at
least ell'_i because U_i>=0, and the current second coordinate is
between0 and D'_i Y_i until the checkpoint. Earlier terms remain in
their post-checkpoint zero region, and later terms remain in their
pre-activation zero region. Differentiability implies that each zero
term has zero gradient. Thus only the current term contributes, and
its factor1/2 converts every physical h_t into the chosen local h_t/2.
The checkpoint places the next coordinate at
ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1). All samples, including zeros, keep their original
physical index. There is no restarted subproblem clock.

In the final block the gradient is delta/2; at cumulative tail sum u
the last shifted position is D'_k-u delta/2. It stays at least delta,
since u<=s_k and D'_k=delta(1+s_k). Therefore

    F(x_n)=(1/2)[delta(D'_k-s_k delta/2)-delta^2/2]
          =(D'_k)^2/[4(1+s_k)].

Initial e_0 and the specified genuine minimizer0 have distance1.
It follows that

    W_n(h)>=1/[2(1+s_k)]
          *product_(i<k)[b_i/(c(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i)]^2.  (A5a)

For k=0 use just the last Huber term and D'_0=1. This construction
proves exactly the source-format corollary with a_sp=2A/c allowed.
It does not assert that0 is the closest minimizer for k>=1, and it
does not borrow the radius from the different max-envelope function.

## 11. Boundary-case checks

For an empty ordinary block, m=0, r_0=v_0=(epsilon,-1) and P=Q=0.
All plane, scale, normal and zero-region inequalities above remain
valid. Adding any number of zero ordinary steps leaves these values
and the actual positions unchanged while retaining their samples.
The empty terminal block has s_k=0: in the max-envelope function
delta_tail=D_k and its terminal proximal point is exactly at the
hinge boundary; in the half-sum function delta=D'_k and the terminal
position is at the Huber boundary. Both displayed terminal values hold.

For k=1 the product after the first factor in(A4) is empty. If both
ordinary blocks are empty, it reads (b_0-1)^2/(1+epsilon^2), with the
radius and function already exhibited above. There is no ell_2 or
missing middle block to define. For k=0, including n=0, the scalar
Huber calculations give the stated formulas; (A5) is1 at n=0 while
the separately weaker(A5a) is1/2.

At b=1+epsilon^(-2), every post-checkpoint plane comparison is weakly
nonpositive, including equality for the endpoint plane. Max ties and
Huber boundary subgradients are permitted by the preceding proofs.
No step requires strictness. Large selected b only enlarges the scale
denominator when epsilon b dominates. It preserves U>=0 and all zero
regions; the epsilon b contribution is never discarded. Large ordinary
steps are handled by the cap branch. Large terminal steps are handled
by choosing the tail slope using the entire nonnegative sum. Thus no
finite stepsize is excluded beyond the selected checkpoint threshold.

## 12. Matched certified constants

Set epsilon=kappa and let kappa decrease to0. Then
c^2=(1+kappa^2)exp(2kappa Theta). The matching parameter

    gamma_match=(c^2-1-kappa^2)/2
               =(1+kappa^2)(exp(2kappa Theta)-1)/2

is positive for every permitted kappa and satisfies
gamma_match/kappa->pi/2. The circular c_Y=sqrt(1+2gamma_match+kappa^2)
is EXACTLY c, and R_Y=sqrt(kappa^2+(1+gamma_match)^2)->1.
Write base_Y=c(1+gamma_match+R_Y)/[kappa(gamma_match+R_Y)].
The source constant is H_Y=base_Y^(R_Y/gamma_match)-1, and
base_Y is asymptotic to2/kappa.
Consequently

    kappa log(H_Y+1)/log(1/kappa)->2/pi.

On the spiral side,
T~exp(d Theta/kappa)/d and
A~c(1+kappa)T/kappa. The logarithms of the remaining factors,
multiplied by kappa, tend to0. Hence

    kappa log A->pi/2,
    kappa log(2A/c)->pi/2.

Since C_Y=kappa+(H_Y+1)/kappa, its logarithm has the larger
log(1/kappa)/kappa scale. Thus A/C_Y->0 and the ratio of the
displayed sufficient choices (2A/c)/(2C_Y/c) tends0. Adding the
source's polynomial floors2 and2B_0^Y+2 to both choices does not change
these limits. These are comparisons of certified auxiliary constants
at the same epsilon and c, not claims that either construction's
optimal possible auxiliary constant has been determined. They imply
no uniform o(1) relative error against B_n.

## 13. Actual finite equal-angle words and their total cost

Fix the permitted parameters and any integer M>=1. Put
Delta=Theta/M and theta_l=Theta-l Delta for0<=l<=M. Define

    A_D=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B_D=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa,
    t_0=0, t_l=B_D+A_D t_(l-1),
    r_l=R_sp(theta_l)[(1+t_l)e(theta_l)+kappa t_l e_t(theta_l)],
    a_D=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D,
    alpha_l=a_D+(1+kappa^2)B_D t_(l-1).

Every coefficient A_D,B_D is nonnegative and every alpha_l is
positive: 1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta>=0 and B_D>0.
Expanding r_(l-1)/R_sp(theta_l) in the orthonormal basis at theta_l gives tangent
component
exp(-kappa Delta)[(1+t_(l-1))sin Delta+kappa t_(l-1)cos Delta],
which equals kappa t_l. Its radial component is
exp(-kappa Delta)[(1+t_(l-1))cos Delta-kappa t_(l-1)sin Delta].
Subtracting it from1+t_l gives exactly alpha_l. Thus

    r_l-r_(l-1)=alpha_l v(theta_l).

Also r_l-v(theta_l)=R_sp(theta_l)t_l n_sp(theta_l) is a valid
supporting normal. Therefore v(theta_l)=Pi_K(r_l). The finite
chronological word (alpha_M,...,alpha_1), starting at r_M, follows
these actual states to r_0=(epsilon,-1). At theta_M=0 its last
backward step is precisely the allowed cap-boundary equality.

Let S_M=sum_(l=1)^M alpha_l,
P_M=(r_M)_1-epsilon, Q_M=-(r_M)_2-1. Then

    P_M=c(1+t_M)-epsilon, 1+Q_M=c kappa t_M.

For large enough M, A_D>1 because A_D'(0)=d/kappa>0. Discrete
telescoping t_l-t_(l-1)=B_D+(A_D-1)t_(l-1) yields

    t_M=B_D(A_D^M-1)/(A_D-1),
    S_M=M a_D+(1+kappa^2)B_D(t_M-M B_D)/(A_D-1).

The required limits are
A_D^M->exp(d Theta/kappa), B_D/(A_D-1)->1/d,
M B_D->Theta/kappa, and M a_D->(kappa+1/kappa)Theta.
Consequently

    t_M->T,
    S_M->(1+kappa^2)T/d-kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d.

These limits are taken only after establishing each exact finite word
and its cost. No continuous-time proxy or arithmetic sweep is used.

## 14. Paid activation excess and the exact original failing row

For the same local support construction, its endpoint plane evaluated
at0 is

    sigma <(epsilon,-1),-z_circ>
      =sigma^2 epsilon
         [epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon-sigma^(-1)].

Thus phi(0)=0 necessarily requires
sigma^(-1)>=epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon. This is also the exact original
optimum interpolation obstruction: at the end of the ordinary block,
r_m=v_m=(epsilon,-1), so the local proximal point is z_circ, its
proximal value is0, its gradient is g=sigma(epsilon,-1), its position
is x_end=z_circ+g, and its smooth value is ||g||^2/2. Pairing these
with the proposed optimum jet (0,0,0) gives

    Q_(end,star)=-<g,z_circ>
      =sigma^2 epsilon
         [epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon-sigma^(-1)].              (AQ)

This identity is in the original physical variables and the orientation
of fact1252b8823bb5a290. A positive value invalidates the proposed
common zero/minimum normalization; it does not make the local envelope
nonconvex or deny it a minimizer somewhere else.

For the equal-angle family define the excess after the full horizontal
allowance has been paid,

    E_M=epsilon+P_M+(1+Q_M)/epsilon-c S_M.

The exact endpoint and total-cost limits give

    E_M->E_sp
      =c[1+(kappa/epsilon-2kappa^2/d)T
                 +kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d].

On the diagonal epsilon=kappa<=1/2, this is

    E_sp=c[1+(1-3kappa^2)T/d+kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d]>0.

Here 1-3kappa^2>=1/4>0. The exponentially growing term T dominates
as kappa->0, proving

    kappa log E_sp->pi/2,
    kappa A/E_sp->1.

For each fixed kappa, E_M approaches E_sp along actual finite legal
words. If a universal additive allowance A_trial is proposed with
sigma^(-1)=c S_M+A_trial, equation(AQ) becomes

    Q_(end,star)=sigma^2 epsilon(E_M-A_trial).

Thus A_trial<E_sp fails on some finite member, and a universal
allowance must be at least E_sp. If the proposed denominator also
retains epsilon b, the row is instead
sigma^2 epsilon(E_M-A_trial-epsilon b). On epsilon=kappa take the
legal fixed local checkpoint b=1+kappa^(-2); a universal allowance
must then satisfy A_trial>=E_sp-kappa b. The subtracted quantity is
kappa+1/kappa, polynomial, so it does not change the positive
exponential asymptotic scale. For the source-format physical word
the same test uses its doubled physical checkpoint and doubled
ordinary steps, because these expressions are local-step quantities.

This closes only a polynomial additive activation allowance within
this same support curve, endpoint and zero-height reset interface.
The endpoint optimum row already forces it, independently of remote
row tests. It says nothing against positive-height gluing, a different
endpoint or curve, or a better unrestricted adversary. It gives no
upper bound on W_n for these finite words. The horizontal slack cS-P
has been paid before claiming the obstruction.

## 15. Finite-word uniformity and the remaining exact-profile gap

For fixed permitted epsilon,kappa, the whole construction works for
every finite nonnegative word and every admissible checkpoint subset.
The constants c,H,A are independent of its length, sums, and maximum
step. This is the finite-word uniformity that survived the audit.

It does not remove the following losses in(A5): the checkpoint
threshold, b_i-1 instead of b_i, c>1 on every ordinary sum,
max{epsilon b_i,A}, and the exact terminal1+2s_k. If all s_i>0 for
i<k, the diagnostic expression

    I=product_(i<k)(b_i/s_i)^2/(1+2s_k)

has logarithmic ratio to(A5)'s right side equal to

    Loss=2sum_i log[c+max{epsilon b_i,A}/s_i]
           -2sum_i log(1-1/b_i).

It is >=2k log c. The inequalities log(1+x)<=x for x>=0 and
-log(1-u)<=u/(1-u) for0<=u<1 give exactly

    Loss<=2k(c-1)+2sum_i max{epsilon b_i,A}/s_i
                         +2sum_i 1/(b_i-1).

For(A5a), relative to the analogous product with denominator s_i and
the same terminal1/[2(1+s_k)], the exact logarithmic loss is

    Loss_source=2sum_i log[c+(c a_sp+epsilon b_i)/s_i].

There is no identification of either diagnostic expression with B_n.
Zero ordinary sums make these diagnostics unavailable but do not
make the actual bounds undefined. Large epsilon b_i/s_i, the number
of factors, and the activation allowance are retained in all claims.
Pointwise parameter improvements are insufficient to control these
quantities uniformly over words and growing numbers of checkpoints.

The unresolved original affirmative target is the quantifier statement

    for every delta>0 there exists N(delta) such that
    for every n>=N(delta) and every h in[0,infinity)^n,
                     W_n(h)>=(1-delta)/B_n.

A sufficient next theorem for this restricted channel would replace
W_n(h) on the left by the supremum of(A4) over its allowed parameters
and checkpoint selections, including the Huber empty selection. The
supremum is finite, since each bound is realized by a legal instance
and W_n(h) is finite for fixed h. Neither that sufficient comparison
nor its negation has been established by this audit. A finite
checkpoint set can be maximized at fixed parameters; continuous
parameter optimization should be expressed as a supremum unless
attainment is separately proved.

The surviving result is precisely the finite-word construction in
Sections3--11, the matched certified-constant comparison in Section12,
and the finite paid obstruction in Sections13--14. The next asymptotic
comparison remains unproved. No formal acceptance, original-target
completion, or authorization for another round is inferred.

# Finite exposed segments: exact all-start profile from signed capture and chronological payment

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. No new fact ID. Original minimax target OPEN.**

Worker max4, project gd-exact-profile-20260926, invocation87 after ended86.
One authorized invocation, controller cap1; guidance127d43aaa435cc97 and
plan1640aa19a7b1069b. This is a complete joint candidate, not a verifier
acceptance. The current assignment expressly gives zero fact_submit credit.
Every new lemma used below is proved within this report; no old unverified
finding is adopted as an accepted predecessor.

There are exactly TWO substantive mechanisms. Mechanism1 derives the exact
signed interval map and the tube, then proves a uniform geometric capture
law for complete children. After that decisive result, Mechanism2 pairs
actual alternating exterior steps and proves a chronological prefix-payment
inequality. Together they yield a candidate exact scalar profile1/D for
ALL finite segment lengths and ALL real nonzero starts, with the parameters
allowed to depend arbitrarily on the enclosing m. The previous legal
upper-wing reactivation is retained and its full suffix is now controlled.

Mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replays,
solvers, optimizers, benchmark DP, formal calls, installations, consults,
subagents and outward actions: ZERO. All derivations are textual. This
report does not enlarge the accepted structural fact to a segment reduction
of the full function class.

## 1. Candidate statement and notation

Fix any integer m>=1536 and keep the SAME enclosing parameters in every child:

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, S=S_m, D=1+2S, n=2^m-1,
    ell=1+a, b=a*beta.

The scalar d_m is an auxiliary input-radius scale; level m is not an
extra physical step. Physical entries h_i, indexed0<=i<n, are exactly
H_m in their original order. Their excesses are d_i^phys=h_i-1>0.
Every even physical index is a leaf ell; every odd physical index is a
bridge of level at least1. No leaf or other step is removed.

For every finite L>=0 use ONE static function

    phi_L(x)=min_(0<=y<=L) [y+(x-y)^2/2],
    y_L(x)=clip(x-1,0,L), g_L(x)=x-y_L(x),
    T_h(x)=x-h*g_L(x).

Here L is the normalized segment length, not an advertised smoothness
constant. Psi_j denotes chronological composition of all maps T_h for
H_j, with Psi_0 the identity. For s!=0 put

    q_L(s)=2*phi_L(Psi_m(s))/s^2.

The candidate conclusion is

    q_L(s)<=1/D for EVERY L>=0 and real s!=0,             (C1)
    sup_(L>=0,s!=0) q_L(s)=1/D.                          (C2)

More specifically, all starts0<|s|<=d_m satisfy q_L(s)<1/d_m^2<1/D.
Equality in(C2) is attained in the scalar family at L=2S,s=D.

For the exposed-segment subclass of accepted fact d8b59c7d92425121,
with arbitrary finite dimensions, strict exposing heights and nonzero
initial point perpendicular to the height vector, the candidate supremum
is also1/D. Every individual admissible finite-height instance is strictly
below1/D; finite orthogonal tilts approach the supremum. Section12 proves
this statement with the exact original radius. This is a subclass result,
not a full-polytopal or full-function bound on W_n(H_m).

## 2. Static function, all boundary ties and exact normals

The minimizer in the definition of phi_L is y_L(x), since the unconstrained
minimizer of y+(x-y)^2/2 is x-1. Substitution gives

    phi_L(x)=x^2/2                    when x<=1,
             x-1/2                  when 1<=x<=1+L,
             L+(x-L)^2/2            when x>=1+L.        (1)

The values and derivatives agree at both endpoints. When L=0 the middle
interval is a point and phi_0(x)=x^2/2 everywhere. In general g_L is
continuous, nondecreasing, and has slopes1,0,1, so phi_L is convex,
differentiable and1-smooth. Its complete minimum set is{0}, its minimum
value is0, and

    0<=phi_L(x)<=x^2/2, g_L(x)=x when x<=1.              (2)

The inequality uses the allowed trial y=0. For x>0, g_L(x)>0 and
0<g_L(x)<=x; for x>1, g_L(x)>=1. For x<0, g_L(x)=x.

For every original query or terminal sample i write y_i=y_L(x_i),
g_i=g_L(x_i), f_i=phi_L(x_i). Then f_i=y_i+g_i^2/2, so the affine
height delta_i=f_i-g_i^2/2 is exactly y_i. The interval projection gives

    (g_i-1)(v-y_i)<=0 for EVERY v in[0,L],
    (g_i-1)(y_j-y_i)<=0 for ALL original i,j.           (3)

For L>0, y_i=0 implies g_i<=1; 0<y_i<L implies g_i=1;
y_i=L implies g_i>=1. At L=0 these endpoint implications are not
asserted: the same normal is vacuous and the
quadratic formula still holds. All samples come from this single global
function; the y_i are never independently optimized.

For a physical step h=1+d>1, the complete update is

    T_h(x)=-d*x                       when x<=1,
            x-h                      when 1<=x<=1+L,
            -d*x+h*L                 when x>=1+L.     (4)

Thus T_h is continuous and fixes0. This retains the upper quadratic
wing; it is not the unbounded one-sided Huber update.

## 3. Same-m mass and product identities

The literal word gives length(H_j)=2^j-1 and
A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, A_0=1. With eta=a/(c-2),

    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j, 0<eta<1,
    S_j>=d_j/(c-2)-1, S<c^m-1, D<2*c^m.             (5)

Indeed c>1+sqrt(2)>2 and c(c-2)>1>beta, hence0<eta<1.
The displayed solution has the right initial value and recurrence.
For j>0 its value is less than c^j. The lower bound uses
A_j>=eta*c^j=d_j/(c-2), including j=0.

Uniform elementary bounds used below are

    12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2,
    0<b<a<1, c*b=beta^2<1.                           (6)

For example sqrt(2)>7/5 gives the lower bound on c. Also
sqrt(2)<17/12 and epsilon<1/1000 imply
c<(29/12)(1001/1000)<5/2. These are exact symbolic inequalities.
The bounds on a follow from a=beta/c.

Let p_j be the unsigned product of the quadratic factors over H_j:

    p_j=product_(h in H_j)(h-1)
       =beta^(2^j-1)*c^(-j), p_0=1.                  (7)

The recurrence is p_(j+1)=d_j*p_j^2. The full SIGNED quadratic
product for j>=1 is -p_j, because length(H_j) is odd. Put

    r_j=d_j*p_j=beta^(2^j)/c, Q=p_m^2.               (8)

Every nonempty physical prefix of H_j has unsigned product at most a;
every empty prefix has product1. The base H_1 has product a. A prefix
inside the first child uses induction; a prefix passing the bridge has
product p_j*d_j times a prefix product of the right child, and
p_j*d_j=r_j<=a. This proves the assertion by induction. The words
are palindromes, so the same bound holds for every nonempty suffix.

For the enclosing m,

    D*Q<2*c^(-m)<1, d_m^2/D>c^m/18>1.               (9)

The first uses beta<1 and(5); the second uses a>1/3 and(5).
The final strict inequality follows already for m>=5 from c>2.
No asymptotic comparison depending on L or s is used.

## 4. Mechanism1: exact SIGNED interval images

For p,q>=0, continuity and T_h(0)=0 show that T_h([-p,q]) is an
interval containing0. Write it[-p_next,q_next]. Then exactly

    p_next=max{d*min(q,1), d*q-h*L},
    q_next=max{d*p, min(q,1+L)-h}.                   (10)

Here is the full regime check. On[-p,min(q,1)] the map is decreasing
with extrema d*p and -d*min(q,1). If q>1, the middle piece increases,
with maximum min(q,1+L)-h. If q>=1+L, the upper piece decreases,
with minimum -d*q+h*L. For q<=1 its proposed contribution to p_next
is at most d*q, and its proposed contribution to q_next is negative.
For1<q<1+L, the unused upper minimum has magnitude
 d*q-h*L<=d*(1+L)-h*L=d-L<=d,
so it cannot change p_next. Thus the same formulas apply in every
regime, including q=0, q=1, q=1+L and L=0. Since d*p>=0 and the
first term of p_next is nonnegative, zero is covered without an extra
maximum. All ties are non-strict equalities of the displayed pieces.

Consequently composing these endpoint maps is EXACT for a fixed L:
there is no filling-in error, since a continuous real image of an
interval is an interval. We use the signed endpoints in the proof,
not a replacement by an independently chosen symmetric state.

## 5. The actual interval-distance tube and its objective limitation

Define Delta_L(x)=dist(x,[0,L]). For EVERY x and h=1+d>1,

    Delta_L(T_h(x))<=d*max{Delta_L(x),1}.             (11)

For x<=0, the output d*|x| is nonnegative and its distance is
(d*|x|-L)_+<=d*|x|. For0<=x<=1, the output is-d*x, of distance
d*x<=d. For1<=x<=1+L, the output x-1-d lies between-d and L-d;
its distance to[0,L] is at most d. For x>=1+L, put u=x-L>=1.
The output L-d*u has distance(d*u-L)_+<=d*u. These regions include
all overlaps and ties, including L<1 and L=0.

Unrolling the maximum in(11) along H_j gives, for j>=1,

    Delta_L(Psi_j(x))<=max{p_j*Delta_L(x),a}.         (12)

Indeed the initial term is multiplied by the full product p_j, and
every inserted1 produces a nonempty original suffix product, all at
most a by Section3. In particular, the image of[-R,R] is contained in
[-v,L+v] with v=max(p_j*R,a), since Delta_L(x)<=R on[-R,R].

The tube alone does not bound the height inside[0,L]. A useful paid
consequence, valid for all starts, is

    2*phi_L(Psi_m(s))
       <=2L+max{Q*Delta_L(s)^2,a^2}
       <=2L+max{Q*s^2,a^2}.                         (13)

Use as the trial y in the defining minimum the nearest point in[0,L].
Its height is at most L. This improves the old forcing-mass large-radius
scale to order sqrt(LD), but is not used as a substitute for the full
profile proof. For a concrete all-depth warning, take L=2S and s=D.
The whole scalar run is in the plateau, its terminal S+1 belongs to
[0,L], and its terminal objective is D/2 although its interval-distance
is zero. A distance-only terminal objective cannot be inferred from(12).

## 6. A guarded long-segment bound at the exact child input scale

Fix j>=1, put d=d_j and R=c*d, and suppose L>2d. Then

    sup_(|s|<=R) [Psi_j(s)]_+
                  <=max{a*R,R-S_j}.                (14)

The crucial guard is that after the FIRST physical leaf of H_j,
NO later query in this child can be above1+L. This assertion is only
for the present hypotheses; it is not a claim about arbitrary visits.

At the first leaf the negative endpoint in(10) is at most a, because
 a*R-ell*L=beta*d-ell*L<0.
The positive endpoint is at most max(a*R,L-a), and
 a*R=beta*d<L.
Thus all positive states after this leaf are below1+L and all negative
states then have magnitude at most a.

Induct through the actual alternating pattern of leaves and bridges.
At a bridge of level k>=1, its query follows a leaf. If that query is
negative, its magnitude is at most a, since the preceding positive
query was at most1+L and a leaf sends that range no lower than-a.
The bridge returns it to a positive value at most a*d_k. If the bridge
query is positive and at most1+L, its negative landing, if any, has
magnitude at most d_k. The next leaf returns that landing to a positive
value at most a*d_k. A positive-to-positive update decreases its input,
because g_L(x)>0. All bridge levels satisfy k<=j-1 and d_k<R.
Therefore each newly created positive value is at most a*R<L, and
no positive state can leave the allowed range through its upper end.
This proves the guard. For j=1 there are no bridges and the first-leaf
argument already proves it.

Every negative-to-positive return in the child, including a possible
initial negative start followed by the first leaf, is at most a*R.
If a positive terminal has any such return before it, use the last
return: its following positive updates decrease it, so the terminal
is at most a*R. Otherwise a positive terminal has a positive start and
all updates remain positive. Each nonterminal query then exceeds1:
for0<x<=1, (4) would give a negative output. Thus every queried
gradient is at least1 and the terminal is at most s-S_j<=R-S_j.
This proves(14), including zero terminals.

By(5), (6), and a*c=beta,

    a*R=beta*d<=d,
    R-S_j<=(c-1/(c-2))*d+1<=2d+1.                  (15)

For the last inequality, c-1/(c-2)<=2 is equivalent to
(c-2)^2<=1, which holds since2<c<5/2. Consequently the upper
endpoint at this child input scale is at most1+2d whenever L>2d.

## 7. Uniform geometric capture through a COMPLETE child

For EVERY1<=j<=m, EVERY integer t>=0 and EVERY finite L>=0,

    Psi_j([-d_j*b^t,d_j*b^t])
                  subset [-b^(t+1),b^(t+1)].        (16)

This is a coupled complete-child invariant: the proof uses the signed
endpoints, the actual finite interval and its height direction. It is
stronger than a tube or a finite recurrence left unevaluated.

Base j=1: d_1=beta<1, so every input has absolute value at most1,
and the sole leaf acts quadratically. Its output magnitude is at most
 a*beta*b^t=b^(t+1).

Induct from j to j+1, proving all t simultaneously. First take t=0.
The first H_j receives the radius d_(j+1)=c*d_j. By(12) its exact
image lies in[-v,L+v], where

    v=max(p_j*c*d_j,a)=max(beta^(2^j),a)<=1.

Write its exact endpoints[-p,q], so p<=1 and q<=L+1. Apply the
actual bridge1+d_j. Formula(10) gives a new negative endpoint at
most d_j, because
 d_j*min(q,1)<=d_j,
 d_j*q-(1+d_j)L<=d_j*(L+1)-(1+d_j)L=d_j-L<=d_j.
The positive endpoint is also at most d_j:

* if L<=2d_j, use min(q,1+L)-(1+d_j)<=L-d_j<=d_j;
* if L>2d_j, use the guarded bound q<=1+2d_j from(14)-(15).

The other positive-endpoint term d_j*p is at most d_j. Thus the
bridge image is contained in[-d_j,d_j] in ALL cases, including
L=2d_j and every interval-map tie. The whole second H_j then maps
it into[-b,b] by the induction hypothesis at t=0. This proves the
parent assertion for t=0.

For t>=1, c*b=beta^2<1 gives

    d_(j+1)*b^t=c*d_j*b^t<=d_j*b^(t-1).

The first H_j therefore maps the input into[-b^t,b^t] by the child
assertion at t-1. Since b^t<1, the bridge is exactly quadratic on
this whole interval; its output is contained in
[-d_j*b^t,d_j*b^t]. Apply the child assertion at t to the WHOLE
second H_j to get[-b^(t+1),b^(t+1)]. This completes the induction.
No statement forbids upper-wing returns inside either child in the
L<=2d_j case.

For0<R<=d_j choose an integer t>=0 with
 d_j*b^(t+1)<R<=d_j*b^t.
Such a t exists since0<b<1 and b^t tends to zero; if R is a boundary
power, choose the interval whose upper endpoint is R. Then(16) gives

    |Psi_j(s)|<=b^(t+1)<R/d_j for |s|<=R.             (17)

In particular, for0<|s|<=d_m, (2), (9) give

    q_L(s)<1/d_m^2<1/D.                             (18)

This bound is uniform in L, including arbitrary L=L(m), and keeps
the ORIGINAL denominator s^2.

## 8. Every core/plateau crossing is captured through the FULL suffix

Call a physical step a small crossing if its query satisfies
0<x_i<=1+L and its landing x_(i+1)<0. If its physical level is k,
then h_i=1+d_k and

    |x_(i+1)|=h_i*g_L(x_i)-x_i<=d_k.                (19)

Indeed for x_i<=1 the landing magnitude is d_k*x_i<=d_k; on the
plateau g_L(x_i)=1 and the magnitude is1+d_k-x_i<=d_k. All endpoint
equalities are included.

Immediately after any occurrence of a level-k bridge, its original
right child H_k remains. For k>=1, (16) maps the landing in(19)
through that WHOLE child into[-b,b]. For k=0, the right child is
empty and the landing is in[-a,a].

The rest of the original suffix is a sequence of larger ancestor
bridges, each followed by its entire right child. Only ancestors for
which the occurrence lies in the left child contribute such a pair;
their levels strictly increase. This follows directly by tracing the
position up the recursion tree of H_m. No new schedule is substituted.
Whenever a contributing ancestor of level l>=1 is reached, the
current magnitude is at most a<1. Its bridge is therefore quadratic
and lands with magnitude at most d_l*a<=d_l. Its whole right child
H_l then sends it into[-b,b] by(16). This repeats through the actual
terminal. If no ancestor remains after a leaf, it is the last leaf.

Consequently EVERY trajectory with at least one small crossing has

    |Psi_m(s)|<=a.                                  (20)

For |s|>d_m, its normalized objective is thus at most a^2/s^2,
strictly less than1/D by(9). For smaller starts use(18). This argument
explicitly permits every later upper-wing return inside a complete
child; it does not continue an unbounded-Huber formula after entry.
A zero hit is an absorbing state and gives terminal objective0.

## 9. Mechanism2: one chronological exterior pairing

The decisive capture result above leaves exactly the histories with no
small crossing and no zero hit. On such a history any positive-to-negative
crossing starts STRICTLY above1+L, so y_i=L and g_i=x_i-L>1. Negative
queries have y_i=0 and g_i=x_i. These are the two endpoint cases of the
SAME complementarity system(3), not independently chosen forcing values.

If a positive query stays positive under an update, then either it was
already at most1+L, or it was upper-wing and its output is
 L-d_i^phys*(x_i-L)<L.
In either case all subsequent positive-to-positive updates decrease it.
Any later first positive-to-negative crossing would therefore have
query at most1+L and be a small crossing, contrary to hypothesis.
Thus after the first positive-to-positive step, the trajectory remains
positive to the terminal. All preceding steps strictly alternate signs.

The concrete temporal pairing on such an alternating exterior prefix is
as follows. Define e_i=x_i-L on positive states and e_i=-x_i on negative
states. At the endpoint of a prefix that first returns inside the plateau,
e_i=x_i-L is allowed to be negative; no subsequent exterior formula is
used there. For each alternating prefix step,

    e_(i+1)=d_i^phys*e_i-L,                          (21)
    e_(i+2)=d_(i+1)^phys*d_i^phys*e_i
                            -(1+d_(i+1)^phys)*L.    (22)

For a positive upper query, (4) and its negative landing give(21).
For a negative query, its positive landing is d_i^phys*(-x_i), so
subtracting L gives(21), even when this final positive point is in
the plateau. Formula(22) is the exact composition of those TWO original
consecutive steps. It retains the shared L and its negative sign. This
is the promised chronological pairing; it never relaxes0<=y_i<=L into
independent variables or uses the old C_m<6sqrt(m) bound.

For a prefix of k physical steps define

    P(k)=product_(0<=i<k)d_i^phys, P(0)=1,
    B(0)=0, B(k)=sum_(0<=i<k) product_(i<t<k)d_t^phys.

These are exact symbolic prefix quantities, not numerically evaluated
recurrences. Since the last product in B(k) is empty, B(k)>=1 for k>=1.
Repeated substitution in(21), or induction with B(k+1)=d_k^phys B(k)+1,
gives

    e_k=P(k)*e_0-L*B(k).                            (23)

Initially e_0=|s| for a negative start and e_0=|s|-L for a positive
upper start; hence e_0<=|s|. In particular, whenever such a nonempty
prefix ends positive,

    x_k=L+e_k=P(k)*e_0+L*(1-B(k))<=P(k)*|s|.         (24)

For the empty positive prefix the same assertion is x_0=|s|.
If the complete trajectory remains alternating and terminates negative,
(23) instead gives

    |x_n|<=P(n)*|s|=p_m*|s|.                        (25)

These formulas include L=0. Intermediate positive states before a
crossing are strictly upper-wing by the no-small-crossing assumption;
there is no unproved branch guard in(21).

## 10. Uniform ORIGINAL-cut prefix payment

Let U(k)=sum_(0<=i<k)h_i be the mass already executed and
T(k)=S-U(k) the COMPLETE remaining physical mass. For every0<=k<=n,

    P(k)*(S+a)<=T(k)+a.                             (26)

The empty prefix gives equality. For any nonempty prefix ending before
or at the end of the first H_(m-1), (7)-(8) give P(k)<=a<1/2 and
T(k)>=S_(m-1)+(1+d_(m-1))>S/2. Therefore
 P(k)*(S+a)<(S+a)/2<T(k)+a.

For a prefix that has passed the root bridge,
 P(k)<=p_(m-1)*d_(m-1)=r_(m-1),
because the residual right-child prefix product is at most1. We prove

    r_(m-1)*(S+a)<a.                                (27)

Since S+a<c^m, its ratio to a is less than
 beta^(2^(m-1)-1)*c^m.
The inequalities log(1-epsilon)<=-epsilon and c<5/2<exp(1) give an
upper bound
 exp(m-(2^(m-1)-1)/(4m)).
For every m>=16,
 2^(m-1)-1>=4m^2.
At m=16 this follows from32767>1024. If it holds at m, then the next
left side is at least8m^2+1, which is at least4(m+1)^2 for m>=3.
This proves the assertion by induction. Thus the exponential bound is
at most1, and the preceding strict inequalities prove(27). Finally
T(k)+a>=a, which proves(26) for all cuts after the root, including
k=n. All parameters in this argument are those of the original enclosing
m; no small child is incorrectly given an independent large-m premise.

For every u>=1>a, (26) implies the following useful payment

    P(k)<= (T(k)+u)/(S+u).                          (28)

Indeed (T+a)/(S+a)<=(T+u)/(S+u), because cross multiplication leaves
(S-T)*(u-a)>=0. This is a uniform inequality at EVERY original cut,
including the actual start and terminal; it does not reset the radius.

## 11. Complete case partition and proof of the scalar profile

Fix arbitrary L>=0 and s!=0. A zero hit gives payoff0. For0<|s|<=d_m,
Section7 proves(18). Assume henceforth |s|>d_m. A small crossing gives
the required strict bound by Section8. It remains to handle histories
with neither a small crossing nor a zero hit.

If the terminal is negative, there cannot have been an earlier positive-
to-positive step, since Section9 shows that such a step would force a
positive terminal. Hence the whole trajectory strictly alternates and
(25) gives q_L(s)<=Q<1/D by(2),(9).

If the terminal is positive, write t=x_n>0 and q=g_L(t)>0. If there
was no negative state, choose k=0. Otherwise choose k to be one plus
the last negative-query index, so x_k is the positive landing of the
last negative-to-positive step. This includes k=n when the terminal
itself is the last return. The prefix ending at x_k is strictly
alternating: an earlier positive-to-positive update would have prevented
any later negative state by Section9. Every positive query in that prefix
which crosses is upper-wing. Formula(24), including its empty case, gives

    x_k<=P(k)*|s|.                                 (29)

All states from k to n are positive and decrease. Their queried gradients
are at least q, by monotonicity of g_L, so

    x_k>=t+T(k)*q.                                 (30)

Also q<=t by(2) and1-smoothness at the minimum, hence u=t/q>=1.
Apply(28) at this ACTUAL k to(29)-(30):

    |s| >= (t+T(k)*q)/P(k) >= t+S*q.                (31)

For completeness, the second inequality is exactly
 P(k)*(u+S)<=u+T(k), multiplied by q>0. Every P(k) is strictly
positive since every physical h_i>1. No original mass or initial
radius has been discarded at the entry time.

For0<=v<=t, convexity and1-smoothness give
 g_L(v)<=min(v,q).
Integration yields

    2*phi_L(t)<=2*t*q-q^2.                          (32)

Finally the exact identity

    (t+S*q)^2-(1+2S)*(2*t*q-q^2)
                         =(t-(S+1)*q)^2>=0         (33)

together with(31)-(32) proves q_L(s)<=1/D. This also covers a positive
upper-wing terminal (q>1), a plateau terminal, and a core terminal;
no terminal-saturation assumption is made. All possible histories are
now included, proving candidate(C1).

To prove equality of the scalar supremum, choose L=2S and s=D=1+2S.
At every prefix mass U(k), the scalar state is D-U(k), which lies in
[S+1,D] subset[1,1+L]. Thus every queried gradient is exactly1, the
actual terminal is S+1, and

    2*phi_L(S+1)=2S+1=D, q_L(D)=1/D.                (34)

This proves(C2) with a single fixed finite L for every enclosing m.
No limit in L is needed for the scalar attainment.

## 12. All-dimensional exposed segments and the finite tilt payment

This section states the scope of accepted d8b59c7d92425121 correctly:
its gradient is X-P_K(X-lambda), and it represents the full supremum
using arbitrary polytopes. It does NOT assert that segments suffice.
We now identify exactly the candidate result for its segment SUBCLASS.

For a nondegenerate exposed segment containing0, strict positive height
away from0 forces0 to be an endpoint. Write K=[0,ell_K]e, where e is a
unit vector, ell_K>0, and lambda=alpha*e+w with alpha>0 and w perpendicular
to e. Set X=alpha*(x*e+z), z perpendicular to e, L=ell_K/alpha,
u=w/alpha. Direct substitution Y=alpha*y*e gives

    Phi(X)=alpha^2*(phi_L(x)+||z||^2/2),
    x_(i+1)=T_(h_i)(x_i), z_(i+1)=(1-h_i)*z_i,
    <lambda,X_0>=0 iff s+<u,z_0>=0.                 (35)

The minimum is uniquely0, so the TRUE squared initial radius is
alpha^2*(s^2+||z_0||^2). For s!=0 the exact score is

    [s^2*q_L(s)+||z_0||^2*Q]/[s^2+||z_0||^2].      (36)

The factor alpha^2 cancels. If u=0, orthogonality forces s=0. If
u!=0, put T=||u||; all orthogonal starts with scalar coordinate s have
 z_0=-(s/T^2)*u+v, v perpendicular to u,
 ||z_0||^2=s^2/T^2+||v||^2.
Thus for s!=0 an admissible start always pays a positive perpendicular
radius. Additional dimensions can only add the quadratic score Q.
If s=0, the scalar coordinate stays0 and every admissible nonzero
start has score Q. The degenerate K={0} has the same quadratic score.
In particular the formal scalar nonzero start is not itself an
admissible one-dimensional orthogonal exposed-segment witness.

By(C1) and Q<1/D, (36) is strictly below1/D for EVERY admissible finite
segment instance. Conversely, for each finite T>0 the canonical plane
realization

    K=[0,L]e_1, lambda=(1,T), X_0=(s,-s/T)

has exact score

    q_T=[T^2*q_L(s)+Q]/[T^2+1].                     (37)

Using(34) and sending finite T to infinity shows that the exposed-segment
supremum is1/D. No finite tilted instance is asserted to attain it.
For the explicit finite choice L=2S,s=D,T=sqrt(D),

    ||X_0||^2=D^2+D,
    2*Phi(X_n)/||X_0||^2=(1+Q)/(D+1)<1/D.           (38)

Its relative deficit is(1-DQ)/(D+1), between0 and1/(D+1).

The generic finite-tilt strict-witness payment remains valid: if some
scalar q_*>b_0 were given, then
 T^2=1+2*(b_0-Q)_+/(q_*-b_0)
would make(37) strictly greater than b_0. The present candidate excludes
such q_*>1/D within this scalar family; no tilt is silently made infinite
when claiming a concrete strict witness.

Multiplying Phi by any advertised smoothness constant L_orig>0 and
using steps h_i/L_orig leaves the trajectory and score(36) unchanged.
The normalization is exactly W=2R with the true initial distance to{0}.

## 13. Mandatory reactivation audit: the old example is retained

For every enclosing m>=1536 keep the literal old construction

    L=1/10, y=21/20,
    M=a*(1+beta)+a^2*beta*(1+d_2)
                         +a^3*beta*d_2*(1+beta),
    r=(y+L*M)/p_3, s=-r,
    lambda=(1,1), X_0=(-r,r).                        (39)

Its first H_3 is exactly
 (ell,1+beta,ell,1+d_2,ell,1+beta,ell).
Define the actual coordinates by the closed expressions

    x_7=y, x_6=-y/a,
    x_5=L+(L+y/a)/beta, x_4=-x_5/a,
    x_3=L+(L+x_5/a)/d_2, x_2=-x_3/a,
    x_1=L+(L+x_3/a)/beta, x_0=-x_1/a.                (40)

Expanding(40) gives x_1/a=(y+L*M)/p_3=r. The negative queries use
 g_L(x)=x. Each positive query x_1,x_3,x_5 is strictly above1+L:
x_5>L+y/a>2; a*d_2=beta^2<1 implies
x_3>L+x_5/(a*d_2)>L+2; and x_1>L+2 using x_3>1,a<1/2,beta<1.
Hence their gradients are x-L, and substitution verifies every physical
update in(40). There is no numerical replay here.

The query x_7=y is strictly in the plateau and its next physical step
is the level3 bridge. The next two actual points are

    x_8=L/2-d_3<0,
    x_9=a*(d_3-L/2)=beta^2*c-a/20>79/40>1+L.        (41)

Use beta>99/100,c>12/5,a<1/2 for the last bounds. Thus the old strict
upper-wing reactivation REALLY occurs. At physical index9 the step is
1+beta, and the unbounded-Huber replacement would change the next
point by

    x_10(actual)-x_10(replacement)
                    =-(1+beta)*(x_9-L-1)<0.         (42)

The new proof never makes that replacement. Its small-crossing rule
applies at index7: x_8 has magnitude less than d_3. The following
COMPLETE H_3 includes the reactivated x_9 and every later query through
x_15; (16) sends its output into[-b,b]. The remaining ancestor bridges
and complete right children are controlled by Section8. This gives an
explicit full-suffix terminal cap, including every subsequent upper return.

It also belongs to the stronger small-start regime: d_2<5/2,a<1/2 and
beta<1 give M<5/2, so y+L*M<13/10. Bernoulli's inequality gives
beta^7>(99/100)^7>=93/100>9/10, and c^3<(5/2)^3, hence
p_3> (9/10)*(8/125)>1/20. Thus r<26. Since d_m>2^m/3>26,
Section7 proves its scalar score<1/d_m^2<1/D. Its actual two-dimensional
score, with finite T=1 and true radius2r^2, is(q_L(-r)+Q)/2<1/D.
The reactivation is a counterexample only to the unbounded-Huber shortcut,
not to the capture law or the candidate profile.

## 14. Audit ledger, literature and exact remaining scope

The proof was screened textually using query-memory, obtain-immediate-
conclusions, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples and a targeted
search-math-results retrieval. Important attempted failure states were:

* L=0: the exact endpoint formula, tube, capture and exterior pairing
  all reduce consistently to the quadratic case, whose score is Q.
* q=0,1,1+L; L=2d_j; |s|=d_j*b^t; empty prefixes and terminal cuts:
  their equalities are included explicitly in Sections4,7,10,11.
* Long-segment upper-wing return: the no-upper guard is used ONLY at
  input radius c*d_j with L>2d_j after the first leaf. The old example
  has a short segment and is handled by the other case, with its actual
  full suffix paid. There is no universal no-reactivation assertion.
* A tail whose terminal is core: Section11 uses u=t/g_L(t)>=1 and the
  ray energy inequality; it never asserts terminal saturation.
* Late plateau entry and reset denominators: (26)-(31) keep S, T(k)
  and the original |s|. No cost is discarded at entry.
* Prefix products after the large root: (27) is proved at the SAME
  enclosing m, and is not incorrectly claimed for tiny child horizons.
* Orthogonal starts, zero scalar coordinate, parallel height and
  degenerate segments: Section12 retains all cases and actual radius.

A Matlas query for one-dimensional finite piecewise-quadratic plateaus
and silver GD returned results on self-contracted sequences, quadratic
acceleration and constant-step GD. None supplies the required long-step
capture; no external theorem was imported. A web background query
returned the existing silver schedule and related literature. The
known exact local Zhang-Jiang text was already read in the immediately
preceding invocation and no theorem from it is used here. This round
continues the same already-audited scalar regime rather than opening a
new literature branch. All new proofs above are elementary and supplied
jointly. Background only: Altschuler and Parrilo, arXiv:2309.16530,
https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16530. This is not a theorem-level blocker search.

Accepted fact d8b59c7d92425121 is used only for the affine-height envelope
representation/legality scope. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 says
B_(2^m-1)/D<47/50 and gives a conditional minimax separation if a FULL
function-class bound17/(16D) is proved. If the present candidate is
accepted, the segment-subclass supremum has that benchmark deficit,
but arbitrary polytopes are still outside this proof. Passing all
segments does not prove a full W upper. No triangle is opened here.

**Candidate segment profile: jointly derived as1/D, UNVERIFIED.**
**Original all-n/all-word minimax problem: OPEN.**
**New facts/formal calls:0. No finalization or automatic renewal.**
