# Growing Newton temporal spaces on the actual critical word

Status: complete draft candidates, UNVERIFIED. No solver, numerical evaluation,
installation or fact_submit. Accepted facts 1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation and
normalization) and b98bb807d514e80d (the exact benchmark and its elementary
envelope) are the only correctness inputs. Every new estimate is proved here.
These are obstructions for specified growing temporal spaces, not an upper
bound for the unrestricted worst-case objective and not the original target.

## 1. Actual word, queried prefix and a genuinely restricted growing space

Fix m>=128 and keep the following parameters fixed within that horizon:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),
    n=2^m-1, q=n-1.

The prescribed queried-prefix steps h_0,...,h_(q-1) are H_m with its last
entry d_0 removed. Queries are indexed 0,...,q; the last free step is b in
[0,2], including the original b=d_0. Write

    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), 0<=i<=q.

Choose a REAL monic polynomial chi of degree r, with 1<=r<=q. For a
polynomial p let [p] denote its remainder modulo chi. This is an r-dimensional
real vector space; use coefficients in 1,z,...,z^(r-1) as its real coordinates.
Let T be ANY real linear map from this space into any finite-dimensional
Euclidean space, with ||T[1]||=1. Put

    x_i=T[P_i],       g_i=T[z P_i],     0<=i<=q.          (1)

The map, ambient dimension, metric, coefficients and polynomial can all depend
on m. Singular maps are allowed. Initial correlations are unrestricted. The
identity P_(i+1)=P_i-h_i zP_i proves the ACTUAL GD recursion for (1).

All queried values f_i remain joint variables. Assume that the data (1), those
values and the optimum jet (0,0,0) belong to ONE convex differentiable function
F with 1-Lipschitz gradient. Thus the initial distance to this designated
minimizer is exactly one. All conclusions below hold for EVERY interpolant of
these jets; no terminal gradient identity is assumed after the last step b.

This is a fixed r-dimensional temporal space, even when the physical image
dimension is larger. For r=m it retains m independent Newton coefficients.
More generally the theorem below permits a degree much larger than m but
strictly smaller than the full n+1-node representation of arbitrary data.

## 2. The full computable all-pair law and function realization

Let J be multiplication by z modulo chi, let v_0 be the coefficient vector of
1, and define

    v_(i+1)=(I-h_i J)v_i,    w_i=Jv_i,    M=T^T T.

Then x_i=T v_i, g_i=T w_i, M is a REAL PSD matrix, and v_0^T M v_0=1.
Conversely every real PSD M has a real Euclidean factor T. With
v_star=w_star=0 and f_star=0, the necessary and sufficient full interpolation
law is, for EVERY distinct ordered pair i,j in {star,0,...,q},

 f_i-f_j+w_i^T M(v_j-v_i)
                   +(w_i-w_j)^T M(w_i-w_j)/2 <=0.       (2)

This is exactly Q_ij<=0 in fact 1252b8823bb5a290, evaluated on the stated
vectors. No pair or common value is replaced by a componentwise condition.
For feasible data that fact constructs an actual function explicitly:

 cjet_j=<g_j,x_j>-f_j,
 H(v)=max_j {cjet_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2},
 F_max(x)=sup_v {<x,v>-H(v)}.

The maximum includes the optimum jet. The function is finite, convex and
1-smooth, interpolates every queried value and gradient, and has minimum zero
at zero. This provides a function realization, not just a Gram representation.

For a Newton basis with roots theta_1,...,theta_r, repetitions allowed,
the basis polynomials are N_k(z)=product_(ell=1)^k(z-theta_ell), 0<=k<r.
Multiplication obeys zN_k=theta_(k+1)N_k+N_(k+1), with N_r=0 in the quotient.
Thus J is a bidiagonal Newton chain in that basis. For nonreal conjugate
roots the original real monomial coordinates, or real and imaginary parts of
the Newton coordinates, give the same REAL law (2); a complex matrix is not
being called a real PSD metric.

## 3. Exact level resonance: a complete growing-span annihilation obstruction

The level d_j occurs 2^(m-j-1) times in H_m. In the retained prefix the
counts are

    N_0=2^(m-1)-1, N_j=2^(m-j-1), 1<=j<m.              (3)

Let mu_j=1/d_j. Suppose

    chi(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(z-mu_j)^(r_j),
    0<=r_j<=N_j,     1<=sum r_j<=q.                    (4)

Since P_q(z)=product_j(1-d_j z)^(N_j), chi divides P_q. Thus x_q=g_q=0
in (1), for EVERY map T and every common metric, regardless of its condition
number or initial correlations. Every legal interpolant has

                    2F(x_q-b g_q)=0                    (5)

for every real b. This includes one independent Newton direction at EVERY
recursive large-step level (r=m), as well as every growing multiplicity
permitted by the actual counts (3). It also handles confluent derivatives:
vanishing to order r_j, not merely vanishing once, is the exact requirement.

One can add a SINGLE zero root, chi=z product_j(z-mu_j)^(r_j), provided its
degree is <=q. Then chi divides zP_q, so g_q=0. The position need not be zero,
but legality says it is a minimizer of F; again (5) holds. Adding a second
zero root is not covered: a nonzero constant terminal gradient may survive.

For the simple positive roots r_j=1, all factors have occurred by the first
central top-level step (index 2^(m-1)-1). The model already reaches zero after
that step. This uses the actual chronology; (3) is the more general terminal
criterion for growing confluent blocks.

## 4. Near-unit roots: quantitative obstruction with growing Jordan depth

Suppose EVERY complex root theta of the real polynomial chi satisfies

                         |theta-1|<=1/(32m).            (6)

Roots may be arbitrarily close, repeated, nonreal, or depend on the horizon.
There is no bound on T or on a Jordan-chain condition number. For every legal
prefix (1) and every b in [0,2],

 2F(x_q-b g_q)
 <=3600 c^(-2m)(384m r^(3/2))^(2r)
                              exp[-3n/(2m)].           (7)

Consequently the ratio to the EXACT benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n tends uniformly
to zero whenever

             r log(384m r^(3/2))=o(n/m).                (8)

In particular r=m is covered. There is also an explicit larger range:

 1<=r<=floor(n/(32m^2))  implies
 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=3600 exp[-11n/(8m)].                    (9)

This closes an entire growing polynomial/Jordan space, not just a direct sum
of individually legal modes. It does not cover a fixed-width disk about one,
slow roots near zero, or arbitrary spectra.

### 4.1 Elementary parameter and product estimates

The rational inequalities 12/5<lambda<17/7 and m>=128 give

    12/5<c<5/2, 3/8<a<1/2, 11/8<d_0<3/2.             (10)

For example c<2193/896<5/2 and
a>(127/128)/(5/2)=127/320>3/8. Also ac=1-1/m.

Let mathcal_P_m(z) be the product for the COMPLETE word H_m. Its multiplicity
formula gives

 mathcal_P_m(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j z)^(2^(m-j-1)),
 |mathcal_P_m(1)|=(ac)^n c^(-m).                       (11)

Indeed sum_j 2^(m-j-1)=n and sum_j j2^(m-j-1)=n-m, each proved by induction
using H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j). For z=1+w with |w|=R=1/(16m),

 |1-d_j z| <= a c^j (1+4|w|),

because d_j/(a c^j)=1+1/(a c^j)<11/3<4. Therefore 1+t<=exp(t) and
1-1/m<=exp(-1/m) imply

 |mathcal_P_m(z)|<=c^(-m) exp[-3n/(4m)].               (12)

On that circle, |1-d_0 z|>=a-d_0R>1/3 by (10). Since the retained product
is P_q=mathcal_P_m/(1-d_0z), put

 E_m=3 c^(-m) exp[-3n/(4m)].

Then

             |P_q(z)|<=E_m,  |zP_q(z)|<=2E_m          (13)

on the whole circle. The damping estimate is for complex z, not a real-axis
estimate incorrectly differentiated across a growing Jordan block.

### 4.2 Legal early queries bound every polynomial image, without conditioning

Write D_r=product_(t<r)(1+h_t). Fact 1252b8823bb5a290 gives
||x_i||<=product_(t<i)(1+h_t)<=D_r for all 0<=i<r: this follows directly from
||grad F(x)||<=||x|| and the actual recursion. All these queries are retained.

The polynomials P_0,...,P_(r-1) have successive degrees and nonzero leading
coefficients, so they form a basis of degree-<r polynomials. Moreover

                zP_i=(P_i-P_(i+1))/h_i.                (14)

Every h_i>1. Thus multiplying a polynomial of degree <=r-2 by z increases
the l1 norm of its coefficients in the P-basis by at most a factor two.
Multiplying instead by z-theta increases that norm by at most 2+|theta|<=4
for the roots in (6). Starting with P_0=1 proves

                     ||T[N_k]||<=4^k D_r, k<r.        (15)

If N_k has complex coefficients, extend T complex linearly and use the usual
Hermitian norm. Its expansion in the REAL x_i still proves (15) by the
triangle inequality. No inverse Vandermonde or inverse root-separation factor
occurs, and no bound on the common metric was imposed.

In the first r chronological steps, a level j occurs only if 2^j<=r. Since
c<5/2<2^(3/2), h_t<=1+(1/2)r^(3/2) there. Hence

                     D_r<=(3r^(3/2))^r.               (16)

This is deliberately a loose legal-trajectory bound. Its exponential cost
depends on the temporal degree r, not on the full horizon n.

### 4.3 A separation-free Newton remainder bound, including repeated roots

For completeness the required contour identity is proved here. For any
ordered roots theta_1,...,theta_r inside the circle |z-1|=R, put
N_k(t)=product_(ell<=k)(t-theta_ell). Direct telescoping gives

 1/(z-t)=sum_(k=0)^(r-1) N_k(t)/N_(k+1)(z)
                         +N_r(t)/[(z-t)N_r(z)].        (17)

For a polynomial p define

 C_k=(1/(2pi i)) integral_(|z-1|=R) p(z)/N_(k+1)(z) dz.

Multiplying (17) by p(z) and integrating shows
p(t)-sum C_k N_k(t) is divisible by N_r(t)=chi(t): for t inside the circle,
the remainder term is chi(t) times an analytic function. Thus it and its
required derivatives vanish at every root, with the specified multiplicities.
A polynomial vanishes with those multiplicities iff it is divisible by chi,
as follows by successive division by each linear factor. The polynomial sum
has degree<r, so it is exactly [p]. The integral identity for polynomials is
also obtainable by expanding in powers about the circle's center and
integrating each finite/geometric series; no node-separation hypothesis occurs.

For (6), each |z-theta_ell|>=1/(32m) on the circle. If |p(z)|<=E there,

                       |C_k|<=2(32m)^k E.              (18)

Combining (15), (18) and the geometric sum with 128m>=2 gives

 ||T[p]||<=2ED_r sum_(k<r)(128m)^k
                        <=4ED_r(128m)^r.              (19)

This proof covers collisions of roots directly. The explicit root ordering
need not preserve real coefficients term by term, since the final remainder
of a real polynomial by real chi is real.

Apply (19) to p=P_q and p=zP_q using (13) and (16). With

 K_mr=12 c^(-m)(384m r^(3/2))^r exp[-3n/(4m)],

we get ||x_q||<=K_mr and ||g_q||<=2K_mr. For 0<=b<=2 the actual final
position y=x_q-b g_q satisfies ||y||<=5K_mr. Since F has minimum zero at zero
and is 1-smooth, 2F(y)<=||y||^2. This proves (7), including every possible
terminal interpolation and the original last step d_0.

### 4.4 Exact benchmark comparison and the explicit degree range

We retain the actual B_n from b98bb807d514e80d, rather than replacing the
target by a guessed profile constant. The bound on the actual normalized
ratio is B_n times the right side of (7). The accepted elementary estimate

             B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3),                 (20)

is sufficient to show this exact ratio tends to zero under (8): its logarithm
contributes O(m), whereas n/m grows exponentially in m. No unverified C54
comparison is a premise, and no inference about a whole-schedule upper bound
is made.

For r<=n/(32m^2), use r<n<2^m. The inequalities log384<6, logm<=m/16 for
m>=128, 6<=m/16, and log2<3/4 give

 log(384m r^(3/2))<2m.

For logm<=m/16, the difference m/16-logm is positive at128 and has positive
derivative for m>=128. Consequently the positive exponent in (7) is at most
4mr<=n/(8m). Dropping c^(-2m)<=1 gives (9). Multiplying (9) by the exact B_n
and then using (20) proves the asserted uniform negligible profile ratio.

## 5. Actual nonquadratic, full-rank realizations in the growing class

The class in Section4 is not empty and is not restricted to quadratic data.
Here is a finite, symbolic, quantitative construction for every m>=128 and
2<=r<=q. This is a nonvacuity calibration, not a new strong lower witness.

Choose a rational alpha in

              (1-1/(64m), 1-1/(128m))                 (21)

such that all P_i(alpha), 0<=i<=q, are nonzero and pairwise distinct. Such a
choice exists: each P_i is a nonzero polynomial, and P_i-P_j is nonzero for
i!=j because their degrees differ. Only finitely many real roots are
forbidden, whereas the interval contains infinitely many rational numbers.
This is a finite algebraic specification, not a request for numerical search.

Let e_0,...,e_(r-1) be the orthonormal basis of R^r and let N_shift e_k=e_(k+1)
for k<r-1, N_shift e_(r-1)=0. For epsilon>0 set

 A_epsilon=alpha I+epsilon N_shift,
 X_i=P_i(A_epsilon)e_0, G_i=A_epsilon X_i,
 V_i=(alpha/2)||X_i||^2.                               (22)

Take optimum data zero, and put nu=1/4. Define transformed jets

 G'_i=(G_i-nu X_i)/(1-nu),
 V'_i=(V_i-nu||X_i||^2/2)/(1-nu).                      (23)

At epsilon=0 these are the jets of the scalar quadratic of curvature
alpha'=(alpha-nu)/(1-nu), lying strictly in (0,1), at points
P_i(alpha)e_0. All points, including the optimum, are distinct. Thus EVERY
ordered residual is strictly negative:

 Q'_ij(0)=-alpha'(1-alpha')||X_i(0)-X_j(0)||^2/2<0.

For epsilon variable each Q'_ij(epsilon) is a real polynomial, since all
quantities in (22)--(23) are polynomial. Define the exact finite constants

 delta=min_(i!=j)(-Q'_ij(0))>0,
 D=1+sum_(i!=j) sum_(k>=1) |coefficient_(epsilon^k) Q'_ij|,
 epsilon=min(1/2,delta/(2D)).                           (24)

All indices here include star and every query. The sums are finite and
provide a computable algebraic law, not a solver oracle. Since epsilon<=1,
|Q'_ij(epsilon)-Q'_ij(0)|<=epsilon D<=delta/2. Thus all transformed ordered
inequalities are strictly satisfied. Fact1252 constructs a convex 1-smooth
interpolant K of them. The single function

                F(x)=(1-nu)K(x)+nu||x||^2/2           (25)

is convex, 1-smooth and nu-strongly convex, has its UNIQUE minimizer at zero,
and interpolates EVERY original (X_i,G_i,V_i). Its actual GD follows (22)
with the prescribed steps. X_0=e_0 has TRUE initial distance exactly one.

These data have the representation (1) with chi=(z-alpha)^r and
T[p]=p(A_epsilon)e_0. The r vectors e_0,A_epsilon e_0,...,
A_epsilon^(r-1)e_0 are linearly independent: in the standard basis their
coefficient matrix is triangular with nonzero diagonal epsilon^k. Thus T is
injective and its REAL common metric is positive definite. The first r
positions X_0,...,X_(r-1) also form a basis, since their polynomials have
successive degrees and nonzero leading coefficients.

No quadratic function with a minimizer at zero can interpolate these
gradients. Its Hessian would be symmetric and would have to equal
A_epsilon on that basis, whereas A_epsilon is nonsymmetric for r>=2 and
epsilon>0. Thus (25) is genuinely nonquadratic on the required jet data.

There is an exact positive terminal payoff, even uniformly over all real b.
Write p0=P_q(alpha)!=0 and p1=P'_q(alpha). The first two coordinates of X_q
and G_q are respectively

            (p0,epsilon p1),
            (alpha p0,epsilon(alpha p1+p0)).

Their determinant is epsilon p0^2. Minimizing the squared distance from the
first vector to the line spanned by the second gives, for every real b,

 2F(X_q-b G_q)
 >=nu||X_q-b G_q||^2
 >=nu epsilon^2 p0^4 /
                 [alpha^2 p0^2+epsilon^2(alpha p1+p0)^2] >0.  (26)

The line-distance identity follows by completing the scalar square in b;
the denominator is nonzero since alpha p0!=0. All quantities are fixed by
(21)--(24) and the actual word. For degrees covered by Section4, (7) also
applies, so this positive payoff is nevertheless asymptotically negligible
against the exact B_n profile. It is not presented as an improved adversary.

## 6. Source audit, scope and the remaining interface

Carl de Boor, "Divided Differences," Surveys in Approximation Theory1
(2005),46--69, arXiv:math/0502036, Proposition7 identifies Newton truncation
with the Hermite remainder; Proposition25 gives the bidiagonal multiplication
formula; equations(49)--(51) give its contour representation. The exact paper
and these proofs were read. Polynomial, repeated-node and real-coordinate
definitions match this application. Sections2 and4.3 re-prove the required
finite algebra rather than importing an unchecked numerical formula.

Downloaded PDF: downloads/deboor_divided_differences.pdf.
Relevant browser extraction: downloads/deboor_relevant_browser_extract.txt.
The local pdftotext command and existing Python PDF extractors were unavailable;
no installation was attempted. Matlas returned no directly applicable theorem
for the two mechanism queries; browser search supplied the exact paper.

These drafts close two SPECIFIED growing-span classes. Exact reciprocal roots
at every scale annihilate, while a near-unit cluster with growing Jordan depth
is controlled uniformly over all common metrics by legal early positions.
Neither proof extends by spectral radius alone to arbitrary metrics or to
slow nodes of order1/S_m. In particular, adding a zero root of multiplicity
>=2 to the resonant class leaves polynomial/constant-gradient suffix data and
is genuinely outside the annihilation proof. A changed generator at each
block is also outside this common-quotient class.

The all-pair law (2) for unrestricted chi of degree n+1 would simply reproduce
the original finite interpolation problem. Nothing here treats that
theorem-strength interface as a solved mechanism. The exact all-schedule
profile target remains unresolved, and there is no new verified fact.

# Three-generator cap simplices: noninvariant starts and whole-word absorption

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN. No fact submission or new fact.**

Worker max2, assigned invocation74, after ended baseline73. This is the
single analytical invocation authorized by TASK.md and master guidance
c0260879488ba7b6. The two selected laws below are equal radial lengths and
unequal radial lengths. Both use three independently chosen positive heights,
an explicit noninvariant start, an actual positive cap multiplier, and the
entire original diagnostic word. Their complete parameter domains are
obstructed. This is not an upper bound on unrestricted W and does not close
all three-generator simplices.

## 1. Unchanged target, scope, and candidate conclusion

Keep the original PROBLEM.md, including W=2R, every horizon, and every
predetermined nonnegative word. This investigation treats the SAME enclosing
integer m>=1536 and parameters

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, D_m=1+2S_m.

Every child retains this m, beta, c, and a. In particular h_0=1+a and
h_1=1+beta. No leaf or terminal is deleted. Elementary bounds used below are

    1/3<a<1/2, 99/100<beta<1, 2<c<5/2.                 (1.1)

The symbol R in the initialization is a vertical depth, distinct from
the objective normalization R_n=W_n/2. Write P_E for Euclidean
projection onto a closed convex set E. All later local scalar and vector
notation is defined where introduced.

For the upper bound on c, use epsilon<1/100 and sqrt(2)<29/20, giving
c<(49/20)*(101/100)<5/2. Then a>(99/100)/(5/2)>1/3;
a<1/lambda<1/2. These are symbolic inequalities, not evaluated trajectories.

The horizontal directions and actual three-dimensional vertices are

    z_0=(1,0), z_1=(-1/2,sqrt(3)/2), z_2=(-1/2,-sqrt(3)/2),
    v_i=(b_i*z_i,k_i), i=0,1,2,
    D=conv{0,v_0,v_1,v_2}, K=cone{v_0,v_1,v_2},
    F_D(Z)=max_(g in D){<Z,g>-||g||^2/2}.

Here K is a geometric cone; it is distinct from the accepted lower-bound
functional K_n. Write C_polar=K^polar. All spaces and all calculations are real
Euclidean. The actual normalized payoff is

    V_D(h,Z_0)=2F_D(Z_n)/rho_0^2,
    rho_0=dist(Z_0,C_polar), Z_(j+1)=Z_j-h_j*grad F_D(Z_j).

Law I: b_0=b_1=b_2=1.
Law II: b_0=B with 3<=B<=4, b_1=b_2=1.
For both laws choose independently

    0<kappa<=a/1000,
    k_i in [kappa,2*kappa] for i=0,1,2,
    R in [kappa,2*kappa], t in [1/16,1/8].              (1.2)

Define

    k_g=(1/2+t)*k_1+(1/2-t)*k_2,
    e=(k_1-k_2)*(R+k_g)/sqrt(3),
    Z_0=(-1/2-a/4, sqrt(3)*t+e, -R).                  (1.3)

All choices may depend on the whole known word and are fixed before running
one static function. Equations (1.2)--(1.3) are an explicit initialization,
not an optimization or a face-feasibility oracle.

Candidate conclusion, proved jointly below for the WHOLE displayed domain:

    0<=V_D(H_m,Z_0)< beta^(2n)*c^(-2m),
    D_m*V_D(H_m,Z_0)<2*beta^(2n)*c^(-m)<2*c^(-m)<1.  (1.4)

The first three physical gradients have the strict supports

    g_0 in relint conv{v_1,v_2}, with positive cap multiplier,
    g_1 in relint cone{v_0,v_2}, with coefficient sum<1,
    g_2 in relint cone{v_1,v_2}, with coefficient sum<1.

They are linearly independent. Consequently neither the gradients nor the
actual iterates lie in a fixed two-dimensional affine evolution. Every
remaining query and the actual terminal are controlled by a whole-history
bound, not by extrapolating this three-point transition.

Accepted b98bb807d514e80d defines COMPLETE K_n, including 1/D_m and every
physical single cut. Accepted 1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives K_n<=F_ray_n<=W_n for
the COMPLETE alternating-ray channel. Thus (1.4) implies strict domination
by both complete channels, without assuming either channel equals 1/D_m.
Accepted 931b9653ce0f7201 gives only the UPPER benchmark comparison
B_n/D_m<47/50. Its full-class hypothesis W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) remains
unproved and is not inferred from this family calculation.

## 2. Full Gram, independence, function, and whole minimum

For arbitrary positive b_i,k_i, put V=[v_0 v_1 v_2] and G=V^T V. Then

    G_ii=b_i^2+k_i^2,
    G_ij=-b_i*b_j/2+k_i*k_j, i!=j,
    det V=(sqrt(3)/2)*(b_0*b_1*k_2+b_0*b_2*k_1+b_1*b_2*k_0)>0.

Expansion along the horizontal coordinates proves the determinant formula.
Thus the vertices are independent, G is positive definite, and D is a
genuine origin-vertex tetrahedron. In particular its three-height model is
not a redundant two-generator representation. Its cone is closed: it is
the invertible linear image by V of the closed positive orthant.

Completing the square shows that the unique maximizer in F_D is the
Euclidean projection g(Z)=P_D(Z). Its necessary and sufficient condition is

    <Z-g,d-g><=0 for every d in D.                     (2.1)

Necessity follows by differentiating the squared distance along each
segment g+s(d-g); sufficiency follows by expanding ||Z-d||^2. Thus checking
the origin and all three vertices checks the entire body.

Add (2.1) at two points to obtain

    ||g(Z)-g(Y)||^2<=<Z-Y,g(Z)-g(Y)>.

In particular g is 1-Lipschitz. The two defining maximum inequalities give

    0<=F_D(Y)-F_D(Z)-<g(Z),Y-Z>
       <=<g(Y)-g(Z),Y-Z><=||Y-Z||^2.

Therefore F_D is differentiable, grad F_D=g, convex as a supremum of affine
functions, and globally 1-smooth. It is everywhere finite because D is
compact. The presence of zero gives F_D>=0. Its COMPLETE minimizer set is

    C_polar={Z:<Z,v_i><=0 for i=0,1,2}, min F_D=0.           (2.2)

Indeed (2.2) makes every defining affine-quadratic value nonpositive. If
<Z,v_i>>0 for some i, a sufficiently small positive multiple s*v_i in D
has value s<Z,v_i>-s^2||v_i||^2/2>0. This proves the converse.

For later use we also rederive cone projection and the actual closest
minimum. Let u=P_K(Z). Applying its projection condition to 0 and 2u
shows <Z-u,u>=0. Applying it to arbitrary points of K then gives
r=Z-u in C_polar. Since <r,u>=0 and <p,u><=0 for every p in C_polar,

    ||Z-p||^2=||u+r-p||^2>=||u||^2 for every p in C_polar,

with equality at p=r. Hence

    P_Cpolar(Z)=Z-P_K(Z), dist(Z,C_polar)=||P_K(Z)||.             (2.3)

This proof supplies the full polar-distance identity used here; it is not
imported from an unverified draft or an external theorem.

## 3. Below-axis faces, including the missing cap normal guard

Throughout an actual GD trajectory begun at Z_0=(X,-R), R>0, its vertical
coordinate remains negative. Every g in D has nonnegative vertical
coordinate, so, writing Z_j=(x_j,-r_j),

    r_j=R+sum_(q<j) h_q*(g_q)_3>=R.                  (3.1)

This is actual vertical drift, not a frozen carrier coordinate.

For the cone, a below-axis point cannot project into its interior. An
interior projection would have zero residual by variation in every
direction, hence would equal Z, whereas every nonzero point of K has
positive vertical coordinate. Thus P_K(Z) uses at most two generators.

There is also no full-cap interior projection of D at a below-axis point.
Let w=V^(-T)*(1,1,1)^T, the normal with <w,v_i>=1. Since

    sum_i v_i/b_i=(0,0,sum_i k_i/b_i),

one has

    w_3=(sum_i 1/b_i)/(sum_i k_i/b_i)>0.               (3.2)

If g is in relint conv{v_0,v_1,v_2}, the residual in (2.1) must equal
mu*w with mu>=0: its inner product with each v_i-g is zero, and its inner
product with -g is nonpositive. Then Z_3=g_3+mu*w_3>0, a contradiction.
The interior of D is likewise impossible. A cap edge CAN be active; the
initial point in Section 6 checks all its normals explicitly.

For completeness the exact possible face formulas are as follows. Put
q_i=<Z,v_i>. If J is a nonempty subset of {0,1,2} with |J|<=2, write
G_J for its principal Gram and q_J for its score vector. A cone-face
candidate has

    alpha=G_J^(-1)*q_J,
    alpha>=0, sum alpha<=1,
    q_l-G_(l,J)*alpha<=0 for l outside J.              (3.3)

It gives g=V_J*alpha and zero cap height. A cap-face candidate has

    mu=[1^T G_J^(-1) q_J-1]/[1^T G_J^(-1)1],
    alpha=G_J^(-1)*(q_J-mu*1),
    mu>=0, alpha>=0, sum alpha=1,
    q_l-G_(l,J)*alpha<=mu for l outside J.             (3.4)

The denominator is positive. These conditions are precisely all origin
and vertex normals in (2.1). In (3.4), <Z-g,g>=mu; in (3.3) it is zero.
At boundaries there may be several valid supports but the projected
vector is unique. The remaining case is g=0 when every q_i<=0. These
formulas include vertices, conic partial faces, cap edges, and polar states.
They do not assert any face is reachable without its guards.

One can also write the general below-axis closest radius explicitly as

    rho(Z)^2=max({0} union
      {q_J^T G_J^(-1)q_J: 1<=|J|<=2, G_J^(-1)q_J>=0}). (3.5)

To justify (3.5), maximize 2<Z,u>-||u||^2 on K. Its global maximizer is
P_K(Z), has at most two positive coefficients, and is stationary on their
span. Each displayed positive-coefficient candidate is a point of K, so
its objective does not exceed the maximum. Conversely the actual support
appears in the list. This is a finite closed geometry identity; the
initialization (1.3) will have a specified strict support and needs no
selector or implicit feasibility problem.

## 4. A uniform region where the finite cap cannot activate

Put b_min=min_i b_i. For two distinct horizontal directions, positive
coefficients s,t give

    ||s*b_i*z_i+t*b_j*z_j||^2
      =(s*b_i)^2+(t*b_j)^2-s*t*b_i*b_j
      =[(s*b_i+t*b_j)^2+3(s*b_i-t*b_j)^2]/4
      >=b_min^2*(s+t)^2/4.                            (4.1)

For a single generator the same lower bound holds. Since a below-axis
cone projection u has support size at most two, (4.1) proves

    ||P_K(Z)||<=b_min/2  ==>  P_K(Z) in D.             (4.2)

Indeed its coefficient sum is at most 2||u_horizontal||/b_min<=1.
Since D is contained in K, a cone projection belonging to D is also the
finite-body projection. At every such point,

    grad F_D(Z)=P_K(Z), F_D(Z)=||P_K(Z)||^2/2.          (4.3)

For a cone-active query write Z=u+r, u=P_K(Z), r in C_polar, <u,r>=0.
For a physical step h=1+d with d>=0,

    Z-h*u=r-d*u,
    dist(Z-h*u,C_polar)<=||d*u||,
    ||P_K(Z-h*u)||<=d*||u||.                          (4.4)

The comparison point r belongs to the COMPLETE same polar C_polar. It is not
a reset minimum chosen for a child. Equations (4.2)--(4.4) will be used
jointly with a bound on EVERY intervening prefix product. A contraction
at the completed horizon alone would not justify finite-cap inactivity.

## 5. Exact products at every physical prefix of the original word

For integers 1<=q<=n let nu_2(q) be the exponent of 2 dividing q. The
literal recursive ruler word has

    h_(q-1)-1=a*c^(nu_2(q))=beta*c^(nu_2(q)-1).        (5.1)

Proof: the middle index q=2^j of H_(j+1) has level j; its two children
have the earlier levels because nu_2(2^j+r)=nu_2(r) for 0<r<2^j.
This is an induction identifying physical indices, not a changed schedule.

Let sbit(L) be the number of ones in the binary expansion of the integer
L>=0. Counting each factor of 2 in 1,...,L gives

    sum_(q=1)^L nu_2(q)=sum_(r>=1) floor(L/2^r)=L-sbit(L).

The last identity follows by writing L=sum_j e_j*2^j, e_j in{0,1}:
each bit contributes sum_(r=1)^j 2^(j-r)=2^j-1. The sums are finite.
Consequently, for EVERY 1<=L<=n,

    product_(j=0)^(L-1)(h_j-1)=beta^L*c^(-sbit(L)),
    product_(j=1)^(L-1)(h_j-1)
      =beta^(L-1)*c^(1-sbit(L))<=1.                 (5.2)

The empty product at L=1 is one. At the ACTUAL terminal L=n=2^m-1,

    product_(j=1)^(n-1)(h_j-1)=beta^(n-1)*c^(1-m).    (5.3)

No word, arithmetic product, or recurrence was executed by a program.

Combine (4.2), (4.4), and (5.2). If the FIRST post-step state Z_1 obeys

    ||P_K(Z_1)||<=b_min/2,                            (5.4)

then induction in the original physical index proves, for all 1<=L<=n,

    g_L=P_K(Z_L) in D,
    ||g_L||<=||P_K(Z_1)||*beta^(L-1)*c^(1-sbit(L)).    (5.5)

The induction also proves that the next GD step is the actual gradient
step of the one fixed F_D. It never postulates a cone trajectory and then
normalizes it. At L=n, g_n denotes the actual terminal gradient, although
no gradient at n is used to take a step.

There is a useful broader consequence, independent of the explicit laws.
For any positive b_i,k_i, any below-axis start, and any actual closest
minimum Z_star, firm nonexpansiveness with P_D(Z_star)=0 gives
<Z_0-Z_star,g_0>>=||g_0||^2. Since 1<h_0=1+a<2,

    ||Z_1-Z_star||^2
       <=rho_0^2-h_0*(2-h_0)||g_0||^2<=rho_0^2.

Thus rho_1=||P_K(Z_1)||<=rho_0. If (5.4) holds, (4.3), (5.3) give

    V_D(H_m,Z_0)<=beta^(2n-2)*c^(2-2m).               (5.6)

The estimate includes an initially positive cap multiplier. A cap-active
start alone therefore does not circumvent the product obstruction.
For all diagnostic m the bound in (5.6) is below 1/D_m, as Section 10
shows. Consequently any three-generator below-axis witness that beats
1/D_m must fail (5.4). This is a necessary screen, not a sufficiency
claim and not a closure of states outside that region.

## 6. Explicit cap-edge initialization and all its normals

We now prove that the whole parameter domain (1.2)--(1.3), for either law,
satisfies the advertised strict transitions and (5.4). Write B=b_0, so
B=1 in Law I and 3<=B<=4 in Law II. Both calculations below in fact hold
for 1<=B<=4. Since a<1/2, kappa<1/2000. Uniform elementary estimates are

    kappa<=k_g<=2*kappa,
    |e|<=4*kappa^2/sqrt(3)<4*kappa^2.                 (6.1)

Put

    g_0=(1/2+t)*v_1+(1/2-t)*v_2
       =(-1/2,sqrt(3)*t,k_g),
    N_0=Z_0-g_0=(-a/4,e,-R-k_g).

Both cap barycentric coefficients are strictly positive. Directly,

    <N_0,v_1-v_2>=sqrt(3)*e-(R+k_g)*(k_1-k_2)=0,
    delta_0=<N_0,g_0>
       =a/8-[(k_1+k_2)/2]*(R+k_g)
       >=a/8-8*kappa^2>0.                            (6.2)

Thus both active vertex normals are zero and the origin normal equals
-delta_0<0. At the remote vertex,

    <N_0,v_0>=-B*a/4-k_0*(R+k_g)<0,
    <N_0,v_0-g_0><-delta_0<0.                         (6.3)

All of (2.1) is checked, hence this is exactly the first physical gradient
and a strictly positive queried cap height. It is not a formal face alone.
The first actual state is

    Z_1=(a/4,-a*sqrt(3)*t+e,-R-(1+a)*k_g).            (6.4)

Let x_1 be its horizontal part and r_1 its positive depth. Using
sqrt(3)<7/4, t<=1/8, and kappa<=a/1000,

    ||x_1||<=a/4+a*sqrt(3)/8+4*kappa^2
             <15a/32+4*kappa^2<a/2,
    0<r_1<5*kappa.                                   (6.5)

The last inequality in the first line follows from 128*kappa^2<a,
which follows from kappa^2<=a^2/1000000 and a<1/2.

At any below-axis point (x,-r), a nonzero cone projection u obeys

    ||u||^2=<Z,u>=<x,u_horizontal>-r*u_3
             <=||x||*||u||.

Thus ||P_K(Z)||<=||x||, also when u=0. Equation (6.5) proves the strictly
stronger bound

    ||P_K(Z_1)||<a/2<1/2=b_min/2.                    (6.6)

This already establishes whole-word cap inactivity after the first query
by Section 5. The next section verifies the claimed changes of conic
projection and excludes a hidden invariant plane.

## 7. Strict two-face transitions and genuine three-dimensionality

For a generator pair J={i,j}, its span is the graph

    (y,eta_J dot y),
    eta_J dot (b_i*z_i)=k_i, eta_J dot (b_j*z_j)=k_j.

The relevant slope vectors are

    eta_12=(-k_1-k_2,(k_1-k_2)/sqrt(3)),
    eta_02=(k_0/B,-(2*k_2+k_0/B)/sqrt(3)).             (7.1)

They satisfy ||eta_J||<5*kappa for 1<=B<=4. For Z=(x,-r) the orthogonal
projection onto this plane has horizontal coordinate

    y=x-tau*eta_J,
    tau=(r+eta_J dot x)/(1+||eta_J||^2).               (7.2)

Its vertical coordinate is eta_J dot y, and its residual is
 tau*(eta_J,-1). In particular

    ||y-x||<=5*kappa*(r+5*kappa*||x||).               (7.3)

If the horizontal coefficients on the two generators are positive, the
projected vertical coordinate is positive. Then tau=r+g_3>0. For the
omitted index l, the exact remote guard is

    (eta_J,-1) dot v_l
       =-b_l*sum_(i=0)^2 k_i/b_i<0.                  (7.4)

Equation (7.4) follows by taking its inner product with sum_i v_i/b_i.
Thus positivity of the pair coefficients proves every cone normal, the
origin equality, and the strict omitted-generator guard. It is not an
assumption that an arbitrary plane projection lies on its cone face.

7a. First post-step projection has support {0,2}.

For J={0,2}, (6.5) and (7.3) give ||y-x_1||<40*kappa^2. Since the
error e in (6.4) has magnitude<4*kappa^2,

    ||y-(a/4,-a*sqrt(3)*t)||<50*kappa^2.              (7.5)

For any horizontal y its coefficients on B*z_0,z_2 are

    alpha_2=-2*y_2/sqrt(3),
    alpha_0=(y_1-y_2/sqrt(3))/B.

At the reference point in (7.5) these are 2a*t>=a/8 and
 a*(1/4+t)/B>=5a/64. Each changes by less than 100*kappa^2 under the
stated error. The inequality 100*kappa^2<a/64 proves that both are
strictly positive. Hence (7.4) applies and the plane projection is g_1.
Its coefficient sum is less than a by (4.1) and (6.6), so the finite cap
is strictly inactive. Also

    (g_1)_3=eta_02 dot (g_1)_horizontal
       <=5*kappa*||g_1||<(5a/2)*kappa<5*kappa/4.      (7.6)

A sharper bound ||eta_02||<=4*kappa follows directly from (7.1):
(k_0/B)^2<=(2*kappa)^2 and
(2*k_2+k_0/B)^2/3<=(6*kappa)^2/3. We will use it to obtain

    (g_1)_3<2a*kappa<kappa.                           (7.7)

7b. The next projection has support {1,2}.

The actual second step is h_1=1+beta. Put Z_2=Z_1-(1+beta)*g_1.
Let tau_1 be (7.2) at Z_1 with J={0,2}. Writing
g_1 horizontal=x_1-tau_1*eta_02 and using the <40*kappa^2
plane error above gives

    ||(Z_2)_horizontal-(-beta*a/4,beta*a*sqrt(3)*t)||
       <=beta*|e|+(1+beta)*40*kappa^2<100*kappa^2.     (7.8)

Consequently ||(Z_2)_horizontal||<a; this follows from (6.5) and
100*kappa^2<a/2. Its depth is

    r_2=r_1+(1+beta)*(g_1)_3<7*kappa.                 (7.9)

Project onto the plane {1,2}. By (7.3), (7.8), (7.9), and a<1/2,

    ||(g_2)_horizontal-(-beta*a/4,beta*a*sqrt(3)*t)||
          <150*kappa^2,                              (7.10)

provided this plane projection is valid; we now check that validity.
The horizontal coefficients on z_1,z_2 are -y_1+y_2/sqrt(3) and
-y_1-y_2/sqrt(3). At the reference point they are

    beta*a*(1/4+t), beta*a*(1/4-t)>=beta*a/8.

Their errors are less than 300*kappa^2. They remain positive because

    300*kappa^2<3a/20000<a/32<beta*a/16.

Thus (7.4) proves the claimed projection and its strict remote guard.
Its coefficient sum is less than one by the already proved (5.5)--(6.6).
No finite-cap edge is silently reached in this second projection.

7c. The first three gradients span the real ambient R^3.

Both g_0 and g_2 lie in span{v_1,v_2}. At zero error the horizontal
2-by-2 determinant is

    det((-1/2,sqrt(3)*t),(-beta*a/4,beta*a*sqrt(3)*t))
       =-beta*a*sqrt(3)*t/4.

Its magnitude is at least beta*a*sqrt(3)/64>a/128. Since
||(-1/2,sqrt(3)*t)||<1, (7.10) changes this determinant by less than
150*kappa^2<3a/40000<a/256. It is therefore strictly negative.
Hence g_0,g_2 are independent in that generator plane. The vector g_1
has a strictly positive v_0 coefficient, while V is invertible. Thus
 g_0,g_1,g_2 are linearly independent. Because the first three steps are
positive, the first four actual states cannot all lie in a proper affine
plane. This conclusion is uniform over every parameter allowed in (1.2).

## 8. Actual closest initial minimum, explicitly priced

At Z_0 the relevant cone projection has strict support {1,2}. To prove
this without guessing the minimum, use (7.2) for eta_12. The horizontal
norm of Z_0 is less than one: a/4<1/8, sqrt(3)*t<1/4, and
|e|<4*kappa^2<1/8. Its depth is at most 2*kappa. Thus its plane
projection u_0 has horizontal error less than 35*kappa^2 relative to
(Z_0)_horizontal, and less than 50*kappa^2 relative to

    x_0^ref=(-1/2-a/4,sqrt(3)*t).

At this reference point its coefficients on z_1,z_2 are

    1/2+a/4+t, 1/2+a/4-t>=3/8.

Their errors are less than 100*kappa^2<1/8, so they are strictly
positive. Equations (7.2), (7.4) now give the true cone projection and
strict remote v_0 guard. With x_0=(Z_0)_horizontal, put

    tau_0=(R+eta_12 dot x_0)/(1+||eta_12||^2)>0,
    y_0=x_0-tau_0*eta_12,
    u_0=(y_0,eta_12 dot y_0),
    Z_star=tau_0*(eta_12,-1).                         (8.1)

Equation (2.3) proves that Z_star is the unique closest point in the
WHOLE polar, including its remote inequality. The actual radius is

    rho_0^2=||y_0||^2+(eta_12 dot y_0)^2
           =||Z_0||^2-(R+eta_12 dot x_0)^2/(1+||eta_12||^2)
           =q_12^T*G_12^(-1)*q_12,                  (8.2)

where q_12=(<Z_0,v_1>,<Z_0,v_2>)^T. All quantities are the explicitly
chosen input parameters. Neither ||Z_0|| nor R is substituted for the
closest radius.

In particular <Z_0,g_0>=||g_0||^2+delta_0>||g_0||^2.
Since Z_star in C_polar, <Z_star,g_0><=0, and Cauchy--Schwarz yields

    rho_0>||g_0||>=1/2.                              (8.3)

There is also no hidden radial clipping at the first cap query. The
horizontal determinant of g_0 and u_0 has reference value
 a*sqrt(3)*t/4>=a*sqrt(3)/64>a/64. Its error is less than
50*kappa^2<a/128. Therefore u_0 and g_0 are not parallel. The actual
finite projection cannot be obtained by radially clipping P_K(Z_0).
This is an exact property of the initialized legal point, not a proposed
asymptotic model.

## 9. Every original point, remote row, and actual terminal

Equations (5.5), (6.6) show that for EVERY original index 1<=j<=n,

    g_j=P_K(Z_j)=P_D(Z_j),
    ||g_j||<(a/2)*beta^(j-1)*c^(1-sbit(j)),
    delta_j=<Z_j-g_j,g_j>=0.                         (9.1)

The states are exactly Z_j=Z_0-sum_(q<j)h_q*g_q with the depth (3.1).
For each j, the unique cone projection is given by (3.3) without the
finite-cap constraint, which (9.1) proves automatically. It may have
any valid single/pair support or may be zero; no future face is silently
prescribed. At the ACTUAL terminal,

    g_n=P_K(Z_0-sum_(q<n)h_q*g_q) in D,
    F_D(Z_n)=||g_n||^2/2,
    ||g_n||<(a/2)*beta^(n-1)*c^(1-m).                (9.2)

Thus the terminal has an exact projection formula and a proved global
bound. The report is not merely an unanalysed finite projection recurrence.
All intermediate gradients and their normals follow from a uniform
induction; no numerical replay, reset, expected face, or projected-history
normalization is used.

For arbitrary original indices i,j, including both terminal orientations,
let f_i=F_D(Z_i). Their ordered normalized interpolation residual is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,Z_j-Z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<Z_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                      (9.3)

The equality is direct algebra using f_i=<Z_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. The
inequality is the full projection normal at j. It applies also at the
initial cap query, whose origin and remote guards are (6.2)--(6.3).
For the actual closest optimum,

    Q_(i,star)=<Z_star,g_i><=0,
    Q_(star,i)=-delta_i<=0.                           (9.4)

The whole Gram of all original positions and gradients is positive
semidefinite because all are actual vectors in R^3; no formal directions
are identified. Translating positions by Z_star preserves these facts.
For any L>0 take f=L*F_D and steps h_i/L. This preserves every state and
the payoff 2(f(Z_n)-min f)/(L*rho_0^2)=2F_D(Z_n)/rho_0^2. This is the
required W=2R normalization. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is consistent with
this row convention; its terminal reduction is not used to discard a row.

## 10. Entire-word payoff and complete-channel comparison

Using the actual minimum and radius (8.2)--(8.3), (9.2) gives

    V_D=||g_n||^2/rho_0^2
       <a^2*beta^(2n-2)*c^(2-2m)
       =beta^(2n)*c^(-2m).                           (10.1)

This holds for every parameter in both laws, with no asymptotic parameter
qualification. To compare with 1/D_m, rederive the literal mass identity.
Put A_j=1+S_j and v=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)]. Then

    A_0=1, A_(j+1)=2A_j+a*c^j,
    A_j=(1-v)*2^j+v*c^j.

Substitution proves the last equality. Since c>lambda>2,
 c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1, so 0<v<1. Therefore A_m<c^m and

    D_m=2A_m-1<2*c^m.                               (10.2)

Combining (10.1)--(10.2) proves (1.4). In particular the supremum over
the WHOLE parameter law is at most beta^(2n)c^(-2m), and its ratio to
the complete K/ray channels is at most

    D_m*beta^(2n)*c^(-2m)<2*c^(-m),                  (10.3)

which tends to zero as m tends to infinity. This is a quantitative
whole-law failure, stronger than absence of a demonstrated gain.

For the broader absorption screen (5.6), (10.2) gives

    D_m*V_D<2*beta^(2n-2)*c^(2-m)<1

for all diagnostic m; for instance m>=4 and c>2 already suffice. Thus
positive cap height at the first query, followed by (5.4), is universally
insufficient within this geometric model, even without the small-height
boxes or the particular starts of the two laws.

The accepted complete channels obey

    1/D_m<=K_n(H_m)<=F_ray_n(H_m)<=W_n(H_m).

We do not replace either complete channel by a selected checkpoint or
assert its exact value. By accepted931b9653ce0f7201 the initialized laws
also obey B_n*V_D<(47/50)*2*c^(-m). The benchmark inequality is used
in its correct upper direction. It supplies no full-W upper certificate.

## 11. What actually fails in a pairwise cap-angle extension

The old two-ray envelope uses both inequalities

    kappa_angle*C<=O, kappa_angle*O<=C,

where C,O are the two cap lengths and kappa_angle is the negative cosine
of their obtuse angle. For positive length generators v_i,v_j, these are
exactly

    -<v_i,v_j><=||v_j||^2,
    -<v_i,v_j><=||v_i||^2.                            (11.1)

In Law I every generator pair satisfies both strictly: its left side
is 1/2-k_i*k_j<1/2, whereas each squared norm is at least one.
But the ACTUAL initial cap-edge gradient g_0 satisfies

    -<v_0,g_0>=1/2-k_0*k_g,
    ||g_0||^2=1/4+3t^2+k_g^2,
    -<v_0,g_0>-||g_0||^2
        =1/4-3t^2-k_0*k_g-k_g^2
        >=13/64-8*kappa^2>0.                        (11.2)

Thus (11.1) is not preserved by replacing a generator with a cap-edge
mixture, even when that mixture is an actual original physical gradient.
In addition Section 8 proved g_0 is not parallel to the cone projection.
These statements refute the proposed generator-pair-to-radial-clipping
extension. They do NOT refute every conceivable scalar or multidimensional
envelope; a different full inclusion and objective comparison would need
its own proof. We do not silently use pairwise signs as such a proof.

A concrete discriminator on EVERY original H_m is obtained by taking

    B=1, kappa=a/1000,
    (k_0,k_1,k_2)=(kappa,3*kappa/2,2*kappa),
    R=kappa, t=1/16,

and precisely (1.3). Every original normal, the strict cap query, the
three-dimensional transition, complete polar, exact closest price (8.2),
and the ENTIRE-word score (10.1) have been proved for this member. This
is not an isolated two-point counterdirection. Its normalized score is
small, so failure of that local proof extension does not imply a useful
hard-function gain.

Law II also breaks a generator-pair inequality itself. For the short
vertex v_1 and long vertex v_0,

    -<v_0,v_1>-||v_1||^2
      =B/2-1-k_0*k_1-k_1^2
      >=1/2-8*kappa^2>0.                            (11.3)

With C=||v_1||, O=||v_0|| and
kappa_angle=(B/2-k_0*k_1)/(C*O)>0, equation (11.3) is precisely
 kappa_angle*O>C, the failure of the second necessary inequality.
All normals and prices remain paid, and the same whole-law obstruction
(10.1) applies. Breaking this angle inequality is therefore insufficient
for this explicit law too. It is not a universal insufficiency theorem
for unequal radial dictionaries.

## 12. Boundaries, open interface, and author audit

The useful new interface is the finite-cap absorption condition (5.4),
with a whole-history prefix-product proof. Equal radial lengths and three
independent heights do permit nonradial clipping and genuinely
three-dimensional changes. Unequal radii can violate the exact old cap
inequality. Nevertheless these initialized trajectories enter a region
where every later finite cap is inactive and the entire-word score is
uniformly negligible. This conclusion covers the whole two displayed
parameter laws and, via (5.6), a larger explicitly stated absorption
region. It is NOT a closure of arbitrary starts, large heights, recurrent
cap edges outside the absorption region, or all higher-dimensional
simplices.

The still-open productive interface is an explicit whole-word law that
escapes (5.4), pays its true polar radius, and keeps enough positive
queried cap multipliers to avoid the product loss. Merely breaking a
pairwise sign, exhibiting a new face, or showing local noncommutation
does not do this. No recurrent-cap law outside the absorbed domain has
been initialized or bounded here. Even a positive diagnostic witness
would leave the original every-n/every-word target open.

Author audit of load-bearing points:

* The first finite cap is reached by all origin and remote normal checks.
* The full-cap interior is excluded using its actual positive vertical
  normal (3.2), not by picturing a planar cross-section.
* The conic and finite projections are distinguished; they are provably
  not collinear at Z_0 and become identical only after the proved entry.
* Both strict post-step face guards include the omitted generator.
* g_0,g_1,g_2 span R^3 uniformly, so the laws do not reduce to an old
  invariant two-ray or two-generator-face trajectory for the whole word.
* The initial closest minimum is (8.1) in the WHOLE polar; (8.2) is the
  exact price. The lower bound (8.3) is used only in an upper obstruction.
* The proof bounds every intervening prefix product, not just the full
  product. Thus finite-cap membership is justified throughout.
* The terminal is the actual n-th state, its gradient is its actual
  projection, and all row orientations including optimum rows are paid.
* All newly needed geometry, cone, and ruler claims are rederived in this
  one joint proof. Older drafts are awareness, not accepted premises.
* This audit is by the author and is not independent verification.

## 13. Recall, retrieval, and resource record

Read TASK.md, PROBLEM.md, the latest max2 guidance c0260879488ba7b6,
relevant shared findings and dead ends, and own preceding handoff. The
old obtuse-cap draft6a60910d7b5ea610 and zero-query-cone draft
21d26350c9141917 were recalled as awareness. Their needed premises are
not assumed here. Accepted facts b98bb807d514e80d,
1fca3e5dd555cb4a, 931b9653ce0f7201, and1252b8823bb5a290 were read
at the scopes used. Some initial broad recall outputs were truncated;
needed sources were subsequently recovered in bounded reads. No missing
truncated proof was treated as evidence.

Skills used: query-memory; obtain-immediate-conclusions;
construct-counterexamples; search-math-results. No verify-proof skill
was invoked because this specific TASK prohibits formal calls/fact_submit.
All new statements remain JOINT UNVERIFIED.

Literature grounding was in active-program/repair mode, concerning cone
projection and the passage from cap-face geometry to whole-word control.
An initial Matlas query returned irrelevant results. A sharper query
returned Moreau's cone decomposition as Theorem1 of Hassan Mortagy,
Swati Gupta and Sebastian Pokutta, Walking in the Shadow: A New Perspective
on Descent Directions for Constrained Minimization, arXiv:2006.08426;
it also returned Theorem7.1 of arXiv:2208.14196. Public web retrieval
confirmed the former paper's title, authors, and local context: it studies
constrained projected-gradient/conditional-gradient paths, not the
unconstrained GD on F_D studied here. Its algorithmic conclusions have
not been transferred.

The first paper was downloaded to
 downloads/three_ray_cap_simplex_20261003/2006.08426.pdf.
The attempted local PDF text extraction failed exactly with
 `/bin/bash: line 1: pdftotext: command not found` (exit127).
A public HTML/PDF open succeeded, but follow-up public text retrieval
failed twice with
 `Fatal error: connection failed: error sending request`.
No missing extraction or unread external proof is claimed to have been
read. No external result is used as a mathematical premise; (2.3) gives
the elementary proof in full. The final family rejection reduces to that
jointly audited cone argument rather than to a new literature theorem.
There was no installation or attempted mathematical computation.

ZERO mathematical programs, arithmetic/numerical evaluations or replays,
trajectory/recurrence executions, optimizers, solvers, DP, formal calls,
fact_submit, installations, consults, or subagents. Administrative file
reads/writes, public text retrieval and integrity checks only. No
truth-store hand edits, outward publication, active TASK overwrite,
renewal, force stop, finalize, revoke, shutdown, or project completion.
Return at cap1. The original target remains OPEN.

# C99: dense anchors still need strong long-step projections

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT. This is actual runtime round 1
of the allocation launched under master guidance `4226374d0649bf55`.
Completed baseline 29, completed ceiling 31, attempted ceiling 32 with
one historical interruption. No computation or formal verification is
authorized. Every new mathematical assertion below is an author proof,
not a fact. The original arbitrary-word exact-profile Goal remains OPEN;
its normalization W=2R is unchanged.

The mathematical inputs are accepted `1252b8823bb5a290` (necessity of
the original directed interpolation rows), `b98bb807d514e80d` (the
original benchmark and same-word scalar lower bounds), and
`1fca3e5dd555cb4a` (the same-word ray lower bound contains those scalar
lower bounds). Accepted `dfde2fa326c908bf` and `e9a55f57461f253c` are
used only for the explicitly scoped comparisons in Section5. C98/`55b569ca6c4c97a5` is context only. No assertion from
that unverified draft is a premise here.

## 1. The whole class, including dense initial anchors

Let F be any everywhere finite convex differentiable function on a
finite-dimensional Euclidean space, with 1-Lipschitz gradient and a
nonempty set of minimizers. Fix an integer n>=1 and a supplied word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)),
all h_i>1, and one actual trajectory of this SAME function,

    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i,   g_i=grad F(X_i),
    f_i=F(X_i)-min F,      q_i=||g_i||^2,
    R_init=dist(X_0,argmin F)>0,   V=2f_n/R_init^2.

The terminal g_n is the actual gradient, though it is not queried by
the algorithm. It has no prescribed dependence on the queried gradients.
If any g_i=0 for i<n, every later iterate stays at a minimizer and V=0.
In the nonzero case define, for all actual transitions 0<=i<n,

    dstep_i=h_i-1,
    t_proj_i=-<g_i,g_(i+1)>/q_i,
    Jval_i=f_i+q_i/2,       kval_i=Jval_i/q_i.

Assume ONLY the following value condition at the short queried steps:

    Jval_i <= h_i q_i       whenever 1<h_i<2.        (A)

There is no support or value-ratio restriction at steps h_i>=2. The
initial point may have arbitrarily many nonzero coordinates, of arbitrary
signs and magnitudes. Gradient directions need not have a Markov law.
Dimensions, affine value reserves, optimum-row slacks, long-step support
ratios, and all terminal data are otherwise unrestricted.

The class is a class of ACTUAL legal functions. All remote directed rows
hold, not merely local rows. The proof below uses necessary local rows
to upper-bound this whole class; it never asserts that these local rows
are sufficient for interpolation.

For comparison with the assigned Markov/support-ratio interface, choose
any included minimizer as the origin and put

    eta_i=<X_i,g_i>/q_i,
    delta_i=f_i-q_i/2,
    z_i=<X_i,g_i>-f_i-q_i/2.

The two optimum rows in accepted `1252b8823bb5a290` give

    delta_i>=0, z_i>=0,
    Jval_i=q_i+delta_i=eta_i q_i-z_i,
    kval_i=1+delta_i/q_i=eta_i-z_i/q_i >=1.          (1)

Thus the old support range 1<=eta_i<h_i implies (A). The new class uses weaker conditions: at short steps it requires only
delta_i<=(h_i-1)q_i, and at long steps it requires neither condition.
Condition (A) is intrinsic to the actual values and gradients; it does
not depend on which minimizer is named as the origin.
Affine reserve delta_i and optimum slack z_i remain distinct throughout.

Define the nonnegative actual long-step projection budget

    E_proj=sum_(i:h_i>=2) log(max{1,t_proj_i}).       (2)

This formula is defined even when t_proj_i is zero or negative. It is
not a count of anchor coefficients and is not a Euclidean radius.

Main conclusion on the ORIGINAL H_m, defined in Section 5:

    V <= ((a_m+2)/(a_m+1)) beta_m^n c_m^(-m)
                                      exp(E_proj). (3)

In particular the ENTIRE stated class with t_proj_i<=1 at every long
step has vanishing original-B payoff and is exponentially weaker than
the accepted scalar witness on the SAME word. This conclusion includes
fully dense, arbitrarily chosen Markov anchors. No sparsity or lower
bound on their nonzero coefficients is imposed.

## 2. The exact local inequality and its two regimes

Accepted `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies the original residual

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.

Substitute the actual GD step into the REVERSE adjacent row:

    Q_(i+1,i)
       =Jval_(i+1)-Jval_i+[1-dstep_i t_proj_i]q_i.

Consequently, without discarding a slack or replacing a value,

    Jval_(i+1)<=Jval_i+[dstep_i t_proj_i-1]q_i.          (4)

For a zero terminal gradient Jval_n=0, so the same inequality applies.
Adding both adjacent rows instead gives cocoercivity on the actual step,

    ||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2
        <=h_i<g_i-g_(i+1),g_i>.

After division by q_i this is

    q_(i+1)/q_i <= dstep_i+(dstep_i-1)t_proj_i.

Cauchy--Schwarz also gives t_proj_i^2<=q_(i+1)/q_i. Hence

    (t_proj_i+1)(t_proj_i-dstep_i)<=0,
    -1<=t_proj_i<=dstep_i.                              (5)

No sign of the adjacent correlation was assumed in (5).

SHORT STEP. Suppose 0<dstep_i<1 and (A) holds. By (5),
dstep_i t_proj_i-1<=dstep_i^2-1<0. Also q_i>=Jval_i/(1+dstep_i).
Using the negative coefficient in the correct direction in (4) gives

    Jval_(i+1)
       <=Jval_i+(dstep_i^2-1)q_i
       <=[1+(dstep_i^2-1)/(1+dstep_i)]Jval_i
       =dstep_i Jval_i.                                (6)

LONG STEP. Suppose dstep_i>=1. The lower optimum row gives q_i<=Jval_i.
If dstep_i t_proj_i-1>=0, equation (4) yields
Jval_(i+1)<=dstep_i t_proj_i Jval_i. If that coefficient is negative,
(4) gives Jval_(i+1)<=Jval_i. In either case

    Jval_(i+1)<=dstep_i max{1,t_proj_i} Jval_i.           (7)

This argument imposes no upper bound on kval_i or eta_i at a long step.
At h_i=2, (5) forces t_proj_i<=1, and (7) is valid with factor1.

Multiplying (6)--(7) proves, with D_word=product_(i<n)dstep_i,

    Jval_n<=Jval_0 D_word exp(E_proj).               (8)

The same product through index n-2 bounds Jval_(n-1). Every factor is
positive. If the actual terminal objective is zero the desired bounds
are automatic, without taking a logarithm of zero.

## 3. The true initial distance and the unrestricted terminal

For EVERY minimizer X_star, 1-smoothness from its zero gradient gives

    f_0<=||X_0-X_star||^2/2,
    q_0<=||X_0-X_star||^2.

For the first inequality, integrate
<grad F(X_star+s(X_0-X_star)),X_0-X_star> from s=0 to1
and use the Lipschitz bound s||X_0-X_star||^2. The second is directly
gradient Lipschitzness. Add half the second inequality to the first
and take the infimum over ALL minimizers:

    Jval_0<=R_init^2.                               (9)

Thus no chosen minimizer is presumed to be closest, and no part of a
dense initial anchor is omitted from the normalization. From (8)--(9),
one immediate general bound is V<=2D_word exp(E_proj).

There is a sharper final-step bound when h_(n-1)=1+a_tail with 0<a_tail<1 and
(A) holds there. Integrating the Lipschitz gradient on that actual step
gives the descent inequality

    f_n<=f_(n-1)-h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1))q_(n-1)/2
       =Jval_(n-1)-(1-a_tail^2/2)q_(n-1).

Since q_(n-1)>=Jval_(n-1)/(1+a_tail),

    f_n<=[a_tail(a_tail+2)/(2(a_tail+1))]Jval_(n-1).              (10)

There is no terminal support, angle, Markov or value prescription in
(10). Multiply the preceding factors (6)--(7), omit only the last
offset a_tail, and use (9). This proves the general final-short-step form

    V<=C_tail(a_tail) D_word exp(E_proj),
    C_tail(a_tail)=(a_tail+2)/(a_tail+1)<2.                         (11)

For general smoothness L>0, apply the result to F=f/L and the normalized
gradients grad f/L. The actual steps are h_i/L and V is exactly
2(f(X_n)-f_star)/(L dist(X_0,argmin f)^2), as required by W=2R.

## 4. The full Markov anchor price, retained rather than substituted

This section explains explicitly how the obstruction includes the
assigned dense-anchor models. It is NOT proposed as a new sufficient
construction or a free Gram optimization.

Suppose the nonzero QUERIED gradient directions u_i=g_i/A_i,
A_i=sqrt(q_i), 0<=i<n, satisfy the product law

    <u_i,u_j>=product_(k=i)^(j-1)rho_k, i<j,
    -1<=rho_k<=1.                                  (12)

For |rho_i|<1, the canonical innovation is

    e_(i+1)=(u_(i+1)-rho_i u_i)/sqrt(1-rho_i^2).

Expanding its inner products using (12) shows it has norm1 and is
orthogonal to u_0,...,u_i. It is therefore also orthogonal to all earlier
canonical innovations. Put e_0=u_0. Conversely the literal recursion

    u_0=e_0,
    u_(i+1)=rho_i u_i+sqrt(1-rho_i^2)e_(i+1)         (13)

realizes (12) in real coordinates. Degenerate rho_i=+/-1 means
u_(i+1)=rho_i u_i and contributes no canonical innovation.

The corresponding joint sign law is entirely specified by these same
rho_i: for s_sign_i in {-1,1},

    P_sign(s_sign_0,...,s_sign_(n-1))=2^(-n)
                    product_(i<n-1)(1+rho_i s_sign_i s_sign_(i+1)). (14)

It is nonnegative and normalized: summing the last sign gives a
factor2, successively. Equivalently take a fair initial sign and
independent bond signs of means rho_i; this proves (12) for their
moments. Strictly interior rho_i give full support. Neither positivity
of this law nor (13) is asserted to imply interpolation.

Let 0 be any included minimizer and w_(i+1)=<X_0,e_(i+1)>.
Earlier gradients are orthogonal to the innovation, so actual GD and
the definition eta_i=<X_i,g_i>/q_i imply

    eta_(i+1)A_(i+1)
       =rho_i(eta_i-h_i)A_i+sqrt(1-rho_i^2)w_(i+1).

Orthogonal decomposition gives the COMPLETE norm identity

    ||X_0||^2=eta_0^2 q_0
       +sum_(i:|rho_i|<1)
          [eta_(i+1)A_(i+1)-rho_i(eta_i-h_i)A_i]^2
                                      /(1-rho_i^2)
       +||X_perp||^2,                              (15)

where X_perp is orthogonal to all queried gradients. At a degenerate
transition the numerator must be zero, and there is no summand. Formula
(15) accounts for every actual nonzero initial innovation, including
all of them in a dense rule. It is the norm about the included minimizer;
it equals R_init^2 only when that minimizer is closest. The obstruction
uses (9), which is valid for the true radius in ALL cases, instead of
silently identifying these quantities.

For queried transitions the actual projection coefficient is

    t_proj_i=-rho_i A_(i+1)/A_i.                    (16)

Consequently a bound on |rho_i| alone is not the projection cap.
Even an interior, bounded correlation can have t_proj_i>1 when the
next gradient amplitude grows. Conversely, any real rule of the form
g_(i+1)=-t_i g_i+v_i e_(i+1) has t_proj_i=t_i, regardless of its
initial-anchor coefficients. Thus EVERY dense modification of the
previous bounded-rule class retaining t_i<=1 at long steps and (A)
is excluded by (11), without prescribing its q_i, eta_i, z_i, w_i,
or its permitted remote rows. No tiny-coefficient support count is used.

The terminal is still free: the Markov assumptions in this section stop
at index n-1. For the original H_m its last physical step is short, so
the obstruction does not even impose a long-step projection condition
on the actual terminal gradient.

## 5. ORIGINAL enclosing-m words and exact benchmark comparison

For each integer m>=4 fix the SAME enclosing-m parameters in every
recursive child:

    N=2^m, n=N-1, sigma=1+sqrt(2),
    beta_m=1-1/m, c_m=sigma(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

The supplied word is the actual H_m. Parameters are not reset when
passing to a child. Its smallest and last step is 1+a_m, with
0<a_m<1/2. There are N/2^(j+1) steps of level j, for 0<=j<m.

The product of all offsets is

    D_word=a_m^(N-1)c_m^(N-m-1)=beta_m^n c_m^(-m).  (17)

For completeness the exponents follow by induction: the offset
product for H_(j+1) is its H_j product squared, times a_m c_m^j.
Starting from1, its a_m exponent is 2^j-1 and its c_m exponent is
2^j-j-1. Setting j=m and a_m=beta_m/c_m proves (17).
Substituting (17) into (11) proves (3).

The benchmark B_n is the ORIGINAL all-split Zhang--Jiang recurrence
in accepted `b98bb807d514e80d`, never a single selected tree. That fact
gives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3)<=1+4*3^m. Therefore

    B_n V<=2(1+4*3^m)c_m^(-m)
                               exp(-n/m+E_proj).   (18)

Here log(beta_m)<=-1/m was used only as an inequality. In particular,
E_proj=o(n/m) forces B_n V->0. The same follows under
E_proj<=(1-epsilon)n/m for any fixed epsilon>0. The capped family
t_proj_i<=1 has E_proj=0, independently of anchor density.

There is also a direct SAME-WORD comparison which needs no asymptotic
estimate of the optimum benchmark. Write S_m=sum H_m and M_m=1+2S_m.
Summing the level contributions gives

    S_m=N-1+a_m(c_m^m-N)/(c_m-2).                   (19)

One can also prove (19) from S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a_m c_m^j.
Since sigma(sigma-2)=1, c_m>sigma and beta_m<1,

    a_m/(c_m-2)=beta_m/[c_m(c_m-2)]<1.

Also c_m^m>N. Equation (19) consequently gives M_m<2c_m^m.
The accepted global Huber term on this EXACT word is 1/M_m; it is
included in K_n(H_m) by `b98bb807d514e80d`, and
K_n(H_m)<=F_ray_n(H_m) by `1fca3e5dd555cb4a`. Hence

    V/(1/M_m)<4 beta_m^n exp(E_proj),
    V/K_n(H_m)<=V/(1/M_m),
    V/F_ray_n(H_m)<=V/K_n(H_m).                    (20)

For E_proj=0, and more generally E_proj=o(n/m), all these ratios tend
to zero. Thus the class is not merely below the requested matching
constant; it is weaker than an already accepted witness on the same
word. No optimization or numerical comparison is involved.

The accepted augmented lens witness V_lens(H_m) from
`dfde2fa326c908bf` also includes the SAME term 1/M_m in its defining
maximum. Thus V/V_lens(H_m)<=V/(1/M_m), and the same limit in (20)
applies. This does not assert that an individual complex parameter
beats this class, or transfer the separate Chebyshev-word obstruction.

Accepted C59/`e9a55f57461f253c` supplies a further lower witness only
for the actual m=6 word with its enclosing-6 parameters. For E_proj=0
at that word, (3) gives V<2c_6^(-6). Since c_6>7/3 and
(3/7)^2=9/49<1/5, this implies

    V<2/125<1/50<56/2711.

So the capped class is below the accepted C59 witness on that ACTUAL
word. C59 is not transferred to an H_6-shaped subword with a different
enclosing m. The other accepted restricted obstructions and conditional
seed-substitution result supply no additional lower witness for this
specified family, so no pointwise comparison is inferred from them.

## 6. Quantitative escape requirements, not sufficient conditions

Suppose a family of actual instances satisfying (A) on these words has
liminf B_n V>0. Choose v_0>0 with B_n V>=v_0 eventually. Taking logarithms
in (3) gives the NECESSARY inequality

    E_proj>=n log(1/beta_m)+m log c_m
               -log B_n-log C_tail(a_m)+log v_0.    (21)

Since m log(1/beta_m)->1, log B_n=O(m), and m^2/n->0,

    liminf m E_proj/n >=1.                         (22)

This is a budget of actual strong negative projections, not a claim
that meeting it is useful or legal. It does not replace (15), the
remote interpolation rows, or the terminal payoff calculation.

The only long levels are j>=2: level0 offset a_m<1, level1 offset
beta_m<1, while level2 offset beta_m c_m=sigma(1-1/m^2)>1 for m>=4.
Thus (21) forces useful strong projections at levels at least2; making
the initial anchor dense at the short levels alone does not supply it.

There is a purely analytic count refinement. Let K_proj be the number
of long indices with t_proj_i>1. For such an index of level j, (5)
gives log t_proj_i<=log(beta_m c_m^(j-1))<=(j-1)log c_m.
For every integer r>=1, the number of ALL indices with j-1>=r is at
most N/2^(r+1). Counting the levels of the selected K_proj indices
layer by layer therefore gives, when K_proj>0,

    E_proj <= log c_m sum_(r>=1)min{K_proj,N/2^(r+1)}
           <= (log c_m)K_proj[log_2(N/K_proj)+2].    (23)

For the last inequality put l=floor(log_2(N/K_proj)), which is
nonnegative. Bound the first l summands by K_proj each; the remaining
geometric tail is N/2^(l+1)<K_proj. Replacing l+1 by
log_2(N/K_proj)+2 is a valid weaker bound. For K_proj=0, E_proj=0.

In particular K_proj=o(n/(m log m)) forces E_proj=o(n/m) and is
excluded. To check this limit without any hidden uniformity assumption,
write x=K_proj/N and y=m log m*x. Then y->0 and

    m x log(1/x)
       =(y/log m)[log m+log log m+log(1/y)] ->0,

using y log(1/y)->0. Formula (23) gives the assertion. This count refers
to strong long-step projections, not to the number of nonzero initial
innovation coefficients. No converse is asserted.

For additional precision, equation (4) itself has local multiplier

    1+[dstep_i t_proj_i-1]/kval_i.                      (24)

At a long step this can exceed dstep_i only if

    t_proj_i>1+[(kval_i-1)(dstep_i-1)]/dstep_i.             (25)

Thus t_proj_i>1 can still be insufficient when a positive affine
reserve is paid. At a short step, (5) shows that beating the multiplier
dstep_i is impossible unless kval_i>h_i, equivalently
delta_i>(h_i-1)q_i. Since kval_i=eta_i-z_i/q_i, merely moving the
support eta_i across h_i is insufficient if optimum slack absorbs it.

The next regime is therefore concrete: either build dense real data
with effective long-step anti-alignment beyond (25), of cumulative
size required by (21), or allow an actual short-step VALUE reserve
crossing the physical step. Changing the Markov law alone does not
avoid this obstruction; Sections 2--3 never used that law.

## 7. Exact boundary counterexamples and scope audit

These counterexamples remove one hypothesis of the local contraction;
they do not refute the proved scoped result or solve the original Goal.
They are actual fixed functions, so every remote/optimum row is retained.

For F(x)=kappa x^2/2, with 0<kappa<=1, one has for all sample pairs,
including the optimum0,

    Q_ij=-kappa(1-kappa)(X_i-X_j)^2/2<=0.           (26)

The minimizer is uniquely0 and an initial point X_0=1 has R_init=1.
Here delta_i=z_i=kappa(1-kappa)X_i^2/2, so both reserves are positive
at every nonzero sample when kappa<1.

DROP THE LONG PROJECTION CAP. Take kappa=3/4 and the one-step word h=(4).
Then X_1=-2, t_proj_0=2, dstep_0=3, and

    Jval_0=21/32, Jval_1=21/8=4Jval_0>3Jval_0.

All support ratios are eta_i=4/3 and kval_i=7/6. Thus the contraction
Jval_1<=dstep_0 Jval_0 is false if the strong long-step projection is
allowed for free, even with positive affine reserve AND optimum slack.
Its actual payoff is V=3. Formula (7), which pays t_proj_0, remains valid.

DROP THE SHORT VALUE CONDITION. Take kappa=1/8 and h=(3/2). Then
X_1=13/16, t_proj_0=-13/16, dstep_0=1/2, and

    kval_0=9/2>h_0,
    Jval_1/Jval_0=(13/16)^2=169/256>1/2.

No strong negative projection is present. The true radius is1 and
V=169/2048. Formula (26) certifies all rows. The short-step value
condition cannot simply be omitted. In both examples the initial
point and one static function are specified before their GD step.

Author audit of the main claim:

* The sign of the negative short-step coefficient in (6) was checked
  explicitly; reversing it would destroy the argument.
* The true radius follows from (9) for every minimizer, not from
  replacing R_init by the chosen-origin norm in (15).
* All claimed whole-family upper bounds are necessary consequences of
  actual full interpolation. No adjacent-only sufficiency is asserted.
* Arbitrary dense anchors, arbitrary slack, and a free terminal are
  included. No Markov premise is used for the obstruction.
* The final physical offset is retained in (10)--(11). The benchmark
  and every word parameter in (17)--(23) are original enclosing-m data.
* Weighted projection budget, anchor coefficient count, and initial
  norm are distinct quantities. Necessary budget/count inequalities
  are never described as sufficient.

## 8. Search audit and remaining work

The search-math-results workflow was run in active-program repair mode
for a dense paid Markov anchor and its full interpolation constraints.
Matlas returned constant-short-step gradient-flow summability,
self-contracted GD sequences, and perturbed gradient-flow bounds. Their
step assumptions do not cover the actual long-step word, and no returned
theorem is used. Web fallback produced covariance/Markov and interpolation
background but no applicable construction.

The exact local paper Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, *Smooth Strongly Convex
Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance of First-order Methods*,
arXiv:1502.05666, was reread at Section2.5, its Theorem4, proof, and
conjugate construction. Its definitions were specialized at mu=0,L=1.
Here the mathematical premise is the self-contained accepted fact
`1252b8823bb5a290`; no external theorem has been newly imported. No new
covariance-paper machinery is needed: (12)--(15) are elementary inner
product identities, and the new obstruction does not assume (12).

This closes the ENTIRE dense extension of the bounded-projection rule,
and a larger class, subject to the stated short-step value condition.
It does not close dense Markov rules with the effective long projections
in (25), and it does not close short-step value-reserve crossings.
The next allocated round should attack one such actual rule with all
coordinates, signs, supports, the full initial norm, every original row,
and a quantitative original-B payoff. A finite strong-projection example
or the necessary budget alone is not that construction.

No new fact_submit, numerical evaluation, solver, machine algebra,
formal verifier, consult, installation, or subagent was used. All
previous computational ledgers remain CLOSED. There is one allocated
analytical round remaining after this round returns; no renewal is
inferred. The original arbitrary-h matching theorem is unresolved.
