# Actual cycle suffix: an explicit terminal floor and the failed flux comparison

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL DRAFT, max3 round49, 2026-10-01.
This is the single analytical round authorized by TASK.md and guidance
4476b1d7b1881436, after completed48, with completed ceiling49 and attempted
ceiling50. No numerical evaluation, trajectory replay, symbolic program,
solver, benchmark DP, fact_submit, installation, consult, or subagent was used.
All arguments below are textual. No new result here is a verified fact.

The requested benchmark-scale terminal estimate remains OPEN. This draft
does obtain the following weaker, actual-terminal estimate, for every m>=8:

    J_m >= 2 [144 c m^2(m+1)]^(-N 2^m) > 0.             (A)

It excludes exact annihilation, including at every intermediate physical
query, but its right side tends to zero far too rapidly to establish even
liminf J_m>0. It supplies no useful upper bound on J_m. The proof applies
to the actual changing sectors and needs no presumed diffusion kernel.

The attempted useful lower bound instead uses cumulative positive flux.
Its exact scalar form is (20) below. Two precise failures are recorded:

* The sandpile least-action proof loses its required emission inequality
  at the FOURTH physical suffix update, the first occurrence of t_2.
  A same-cycle test state explicitly violates the proposed flux barrier.
* Even granting the unproved favorable bound M_k<=M_0 at all times, the
  resulting total-clock estimate inserted in (20) is asymptotically zero.
  The constants in (22)--(23) show this without numerical evaluation.

The full starting manuscript is shared finding37ec6ea9a52bfbdf, the
editorially clarified successor to the TASK snapshot81c766c8e8e55ca5.
Its public file SHA256 is
a59effa1d6ff0c1ef15ced41f7eedc4b6fbc9028f5bcd0f5eab1cf5740906a0d.
It is motivation and an object specification, NOT a mathematical predecessor.
Every preliminary formula used here is rederived within this joint draft.
The only verified external-to-this-draft input used in the comparison is
fact04219554e41d9750's unconditional original-benchmark ray limit.

## 1. The unchanged object and its actual terminal normalization

Fix the enclosing integer m>=8 and define

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
    t_j=1+a c^j, 0<=j<m,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,t_j,H_j),
    d=t_(m-1)=1+beta c^(m-2),
    N=ceil(pi sqrt(d/6)), theta=pi/(2N), mu=sin(theta)^2,
    z=cos(theta), v_i=cos((2i+1)theta), 0<=i<N.

Every child uses this same m. Indices on vectors are cyclic modulo N. Put

    (D x)_i=x_i-x_(i+1), L_e=D D^T,
    F(x)=||(D x)_+||^2/8,
    T_t(x)=x-(t/4)D^T(D x)_+.

Here s_+=max(s,0), used componentwise on vectors; norms and inner
products are Euclidean, and I denotes an identity matrix of the required size.

The declared smoothness remains 1. Indeed, the derivative of s_+^2/2 is
s_+, so grad F(x)=D^T(Dx)_+/4. The positive-part map is 1-Lipschitz and
sum_i(x_i-x_(i+1))^2<=4 sum_i x_i^2; hence grad F is 1-Lipschitz.
Convexity follows by summing the convex scalar functions. The minimum is
zero and its set is exactly the constant vectors, since all inequalities
x_i<=x_(i+1) on a cycle force equality. The mean is preserved by T_t.

Pairing i and N-1-i gives sum v_i=0. Also sum cos((4i+2)theta)=0:
multiply the sum by 2 sin(2theta) and telescope the corresponding sine
differences. Applying cos^2 u=(1+cos(2u))/2 gives

    R^2=dist(v,argmin F)^2=||v||^2=N/2.                 (1)

The function retains every interpolation constraint. For any two points
x,w, write r=(Dx)_+, s=(Dw)_+. The original directed residual is

    F(x)-F(w)+<grad F(x),w-x>+||grad F(x)-grad F(w)||^2/2
      =-sum_i r_i(-(Dw)_i)_+/4
       -sum_i[(r_i-s_i)+(r_(i+1)-s_(i+1))]^2/32 <=0.   (2)

For a derivation, the scalar identity for phi(u)=u_+^2/2 is
phi(u)-phi(v)+u_+(v-u)+(u_+-v_+)^2/2=-u_+(-v)_+.
Sum it at u=(Dx)_i/2, v=(Dw)_i/2. Correct its squared norm to the gradient
norm by subtracting one half of the quadratic form of I-DD^T/4. That
quadratic form at a vector q is sum_i(q_i+q_(i+1))^2/4, which gives (2).
Thus (2) includes optimum, terminal, nonlocal, and tie rows; no row is deleted.

On the positive ray of v the active graph is the path on vertices0,...,N-1.
Substitution of the cosine addition formula at the interior and the
one-neighbor formula at each endpoint gives grad F(v)=mu v. The elementary
rounding estimates needed for the first child are

    d>144, theta<1/8, 5/4<mu d<3/2,
    mu t_j<2/3 for j<=m-2.                             (3)

Here lambda>12/5, beta>=7/8, and (12/5)^3>13 give
d>1+(7/8)13^2>144. If b=pi sqrt(d/6), then b>sqrt d>12 and
b<=N<b+1<(13/12)b. Thus theta^2<1/96 and
sin(theta)/theta>=1-theta^2/6>99/100. Consequently

    mu d<3/2,
    mu d>(3/2)(12/13)^2(99/100)^2>5/4.

The last comparison is the integer inequality2117016>2112500.
Finally t_(m-2)/d=1/c+(1-1/c)/d<5/12+1/144=61/144,
so mu t_(m-2)<61/96<2/3. All earlier steps are smaller.

Every physical step of the first child therefore stays on the positive ray.
Using its actual level multiplicities gives

    A_m=product_(j=0)^(m-2)(1-mu t_j)^(2^(m-2-j))>0,
    x_after_first_child=A_m v,
    x_after_central_step=-b_m v,
    b_m=A_m(mu d-1)>0.                                 (4)

Positive homogeneity, with the positive scalar b_m, now factors that common
amplitude out of the suffix. Let X_0=-v and let X_k follow the complete
physical word H_(m-1). Define

    ell_m=2^(m-1)-1, y_k=D X_k, r_k=(y_k)_+,
    M_k=sum_i r_(k,i),
    J_m=N||r_(ell_m)||^2.

The actual complete original word has length2^m-1, terminal point
b_m X_(ell_m), and payoff

    V_m=2F(b_m X_(ell_m))/R^2=b_m^2 J_m/(2N^2).        (5)

No start, graph, scale, radius, declared smoothness, or enclosing m has
been changed in this definition of the actual suffix.

## 2. Exact positive-mass accounting, including new negative mass

At physical step h_k write alpha_k=h_k/4. Differentiating the actual vertex
update gives, without a sector assumption,

    y_(k+1)=y_k-alpha_k L_e r_k,
    y'_(i)=y_i-2alpha r_i+alpha(r_(i-1)+r_(i+1)).      (6)

At ties r_i=0. Formula (6) preserves sum y_i=0. Let
n_i=(-y_i)_+ and w_i=alpha(r_(i-1)+r_(i+1)). For one step define

    O_alpha(y)=sum_(i:y_i>0) [(2alpha-1)r_i-w_i]_+,
    C_alpha(y)=sum_(i:y_i<=0) min(n_i,w_i).

Then the exact mass identity is

    M(y')=M(y)+O_alpha(y)-C_alpha(y).                  (7)

To prove it, on an old positive coordinate the new value is
(1-2alpha)r_i+w_i; its new negative part is exactly the corresponding
summand of O_alpha. On an old nonpositive coordinate its new negative
part is (n_i-w_i)_+=n_i-min(n_i,w_i). Sum these negative parts and use
sum y'=sum y=0 to identify total positive and negative masses.

Thus the obstruction to a purely dissipative mass argument is a specific
quantity O_alpha, not an unspecified change of sector. If alpha<=1/2,
O_alpha=0. On the specified schedule

    1<t_0<3/2, t_1=2-1/m<2,
    t_2=1+lambda(1-1/m^2)>3.                           (8)

For the last inequality, lambda>12/5 and 1-1/m^2>=63/64 yield a product
greater than2. Bounds (8) also show that the stable and overstepping
regimes are both actually present.

Equation (7) alone does not bound the aggregate O contributions. Treating
them as zero without proof would replace the actual system by another one.

## 3. Cumulative flux and the exact first failed least-action hypothesis

Define the coordinatewise nondecreasing cumulative flux

    q_k=sum_(u<k) alpha_u r_u, q_0=0.

Telescoping (6) gives

    y_k=y_0-L_e q_k, X_k=-v-D^T q_k.                  (9)

There is an explicit nonnegative comparison flux for this actual start.
Set s_N=1 for N even and s_N=cos(theta) for N odd, and put

    q*_i=[s_N-sin((i+1)pi/N)]/[2 sin(theta)].          (10)

The grid maximum of sin(k pi/N) is s_N, so min q*=0. Sine subtraction
gives q*_i-q*_(i-1)=-v_i, including the cyclic boundary. Hence

    D^T q*=-v, L_e q*=y_0.                            (11)

An attractive attempted bound is q_k<=q* for every k. For steps h<=2
it follows by a first-crossing induction whenever q_k<=q*: if
e=q*-q_k>=0, then y_k=L_e e and

    q_(k+1,i)-q_(k,i)=alpha(L_e e)_i,+ <=2alpha e_i<=e_i.

At the fourth physical suffix update this proof's required emission
bound fails on the ACTUAL run. Its first four steps are

    t_0,t_1,t_0,t_2.

The initially positive closing edge has y_(0,N-1)=2z. During a step
h<2 an old positive coordinate stays at least (1-h/2) times its old
value, by (6). Just before t_2, therefore,

    r_(3,N-1)>=2z(1-t_0/2)^2(1-t_1/2)>0.             (12)

At t_2 the emitted mass to EACH neighbor is (t_2/4)r_(3,N-1), strictly
greater than r_(3,N-1)/2. The least-action induction requires an emission
at most half the current positive mass per neighbor. Its hypothesis thus
first fails at that precise physical update. This does not by itself prove
that q_4 exceeds the particular barrier (10); it identifies the exact
missing premise in the attempted proof, not a fictitious barrier violation.

The universal version of the barrier assertion is false on the SAME
N-cycle and the SAME update. For this diagnostic only, take an edge unit
vector e and the initial edge vector y=L_e e, having entries2,-1,-1 at
that edge and its two neighbors. It is legal edge data: it equals D x
with x=D^T e, which has mean zero. The nonnegative flux barrier is e.
At step t_2 the actual positive-part recurrence gives

    q_1=(t_2/2)e>e at its supported coordinate,
    y_1=(1-t_2/2)L_e e.                               (13)

This counterexample tests a proposed uniform transport lemma. It does
NOT replace -v as the initial point of the family being estimated.
The level3 step satisfies
t_3=1+lambda^2(1+1/m-1/m^2-1/m^3)>1+lambda^2>4.
Likewise at this actual step SIZE t_3, the test vector has
M(y_1)=t_3-2>2=M(y). Thus global positive-mass monotonicity is false
for the recurrence at step sizes occurring in the specified word.
No claim is made that this test vector occurs in the specified trajectory.

Literature context only: Levine, Murugan, Peres, and Ugurcan,
*The divisible sandpile at critical density*, arXiv:1501.07258v2 (2015),
Definition2.2 and Proposition2.5. Their state is s=1+y, their Laplacian
is -L_e, and their odometer is our q. On the degree-two cycle their
legal-transfer assumption is delta q_i<=y_i,+/2. Their first-crossing
proof of least action uses that inequality before comparing Laplacians.
The actual update violates it in (12). We reproduce the relevant induction
above, import no sandpile theorem, and do not infer failure of all possible
flux comparisons. The exact PDF was downloaded and its definitions and
proof were read through web PDF text extraction. The unavailable local
pdftotext command returned exit127; no installation was attempted.

## 4. A quantitative flux bound and why its total-clock closure is empty

For any mean-zero vector X, let M=sum_i(DX)_i,+,
J=N||(DX)_+||^2, and osc(X)=max_i X_i-min_i X_i. A directed path from a maximizing vertex to a minimizing
vertex shows osc(X)<=M. The mean-zero condition then gives |X_i|<=M,
and Cauchy--Schwarz gives

    M<=sqrt(J).                                      (14)

At the actual terminal time put e=q*-q_(ell_m). From (9)--(11),
D^T e=X_(ell_m). Thus neighboring values of e differ by at most M_(ell_m).
Two vertices of the edge cycle are at distance at most floor(N/2), so

    osc(e)<=floor(N/2)M_(ell_m).                      (15)

At an index minimizing q*, e_i=-q_(ell_m,i)<=0. Equations (14)--(15)
therefore imply, at every coordinate,

    q_(ell_m,i)>=q*_i-floor(N/2)sqrt(J_m).            (16)

The finite sine sum is sum_(k=1)^N sin(k pi/N)=cot(theta): multiply it
by 2 sin(theta), telescope cosine differences, and use Ntheta=pi/2.
Consequently the explicit amount of comparison flux is

    Q*_m=sum_i q*_i=[N s_N-cot(theta)]/[2 sin(theta)]. (17)

The actual total emitted flux is

    Q_m=sum_i q_(ell_m,i)=(1/4)sum_(k<ell_m) h_k M_k. (18)

Summing (16) yields

    Q_m>=Q*_m-N floor(N/2)sqrt(J_m),                  (19)

and hence the quantitative transport estimate

    J_m >= [(Q*_m-Q_m)_+/(N floor(N/2))]^2.           (20)

This is a proved scalar consequence of actual chronological transport.
It is NOT an explicit lower bound in m until Q_m is usefully bounded.
It is recorded with that limitation, not as a solution of the terminal
estimate or merely as the original nonlinear recurrence rewritten.

Consider trying to close (20) using only the total clock and the favorable
extra assumption M_k<=M_0=2z at every time. This assumption has NOT been
proved for the specified run. Even granting it gives only

    Q_m<=z S_m/2,
    S_m=sum H_(m-1)
       =2^(m-1)-1+[a/(c-2)](c^(m-1)-2^(m-1)).       (21)

The sum follows by summing t_j with multiplicity2^(m-2-j). Since
d/lambda^m->exp(1)/lambda^2, c->lambda, and 2/lambda<1,

    N^2/d->pi^2/6, S_m/d->1/(lambda-2)=lambda,
    Q*_m/N^2->(pi-2)/pi^2,
    z S_m/(2N^2)->3lambda/pi^2.                       (22)

The first limit for d follows from
(1-1/m)(1+1/m)^(m-2)->exp(1); the ceiling error in N is negligible.
The flux limit uses sin(theta)/theta->1 and s_N->1. In particular,

    lim (Q*_m-z S_m/2)/N^2
      =(pi-2-3lambda)/pi^2<0,                        (23)

because pi<4 and lambda>2. Therefore this concrete attempted clock
closure of (20) is eventually exactly the lower bound0. Proving mere
mass nonincrease would not repair it. A useful application needs a
strictly stronger, chronological or spatial estimate on the weighted
mass history, or a different terminal functional.

Support alone has a separate quantitative limitation. Positive edges can
advance by at most one graph edge per physical step, directly from (6).
But ell_m/N is asymptotic to a positive constant times
(2/sqrt(lambda))^m and tends to infinity. Its resulting light cone covers
the entire cycle well before the terminal index. It gives no surviving
unreached terminal edge. This statement does not assert actual activation
of every edge.

## 5. Every actual step avoids every sector's exact annihilation resonance

This section and the next jointly prove the explicit but very small
terminal floor (A). They do not depend on the unproved mass assumption.

For a vertex vector X, let B_X be the ordinary integer graph Laplacian
of the strictly active cycle edges, those with (DX)_i>0. Then

    grad F(X)=B_X X/4, T_t(X)=(I-t B_X/4)X.           (24)

The symmetric matrix B_X has integer entries and spectrum in [0,4].
Indeed its quadratic form is a sum over a subset of cycle-edge squares,
between zero and4||X||^2. Ties simply omit zero-contributing edges.

We show the stronger statement: for any symmetric integer matrix B with
spectrum in [0,4], I-t_j B/4 is nonsingular for EVERY j>=0 with the
specified m>=8 and the same formula t_j. For use on H_m, only j<m is needed.

Write sigma for the field map sqrt(2)->-sqrt(2). Each t_j belongs to
Q(sqrt(2)). If I-t_j B/4 were singular, w=4/t_j would be an eigenvalue
of B. It is a root of the monic integer characteristic polynomial.
If w is rational, the rational-root argument makes w an integer. If w
is irrational in Q(sqrt(2)), sigma(w) must also be a root, and

    w sigma(w)=16/[t_j sigma(t_j)] must be an integer. (25)

Here is the elementary integrality justification in (25). The monic
minimal polynomial of w over Q divides the integer characteristic
polynomial. It has integer coefficients: write that minimal polynomial
and its complementary factor as rational multiples of primitive integer
polynomials. A product of primitive integer polynomials is primitive,
since otherwise reduction modulo a common prime would have zero product
of nonzero polynomials. The monic integer characteristic polynomial is
primitive. Comparing contents and leading coefficients therefore forces
the monic minimal polynomial to have integer coefficients. Its constant
coefficient is w sigma(w). This also justifies the rational-root case.

All the possibilities can now be excluded using short exact inequalities.

* j=0: put r=(m-1)/(m+1). Then
  t_0=1+r(sqrt(2)-1) and sigma(t_0)=1-r lambda<0,
  since r>=7/9 and lambda>2. Hence sigma(w)<0, impossible for an
  eigenvalue of B.

* j=1: t_1=2-1/m and w=4m/(2m-1) is strictly between2 and3.
  It is rational and is not an integer, also impossible.

* Even j>=2: put rho=(1+1/m)/lambda, which lies in (0,1). Then
  sigma(t_j)=1-beta rho^(j-1) is strictly between0 and1.
  Therefore sigma(w)>4, outside the spectrum of B.

* j=3: put s=beta(1+1/m)^2=1+1/m-1/m^2-1/m^3.
  For m>=8, 1<s<9/8. Since lambda^2+(1-sqrt(2))^2=6 and
  lambda^2(1-sqrt(2))^2=1,

      t_3 sigma(t_3)=1+6s+s^2 lies strictly between8 and16.

  Its upper bound follows from 1+6(9/8)+(9/8)^2=577/64<16.
  The number in (25) then lies strictly between1 and2, not an integer.

* Odd j>=5: sigma(t_j)>1 and
  t_j>=1+(7/8)lambda^4>16. For the last inequality,
  lambda^2=3+2sqrt(2)>5, so 1+(7/8)lambda^4>1+175/8>16.
  Thus (25) lies strictly between0 and1, also not an integer.

In all irrational cases t_j is indeed irrational: the nonzero rational
coefficient multiplies a power of 1+sqrt(2) with nonzero sqrt(2) component.
These arguments prove nonsingularity, uniformly over every sector and tie
pattern, without computing its spectrum or following the trajectory.
In particular a nonconstant state on this mean-preserving cycle cannot
be mapped to a constant state by any of the specified steps. The next
section supplies an explicit quantitative version rather than stopping
at qualitative nonannihilation.

## 6. Determinant separation and the actual terminal lower bound

For j>=0 choose the explicit positive integer denominator

    Q_0=m+1, Q_j=m^j for j>=1.                        (26)

Then Q_j t_j belongs to Z[sqrt(2)]. For j>=1 this follows from
t_j=1+(m-1)(m+1)^(j-1)lambda^(j-1)/m^j; the j=0 expression is in
Section5. Also

    |sigma(t_j)|<2                                    (27)

for all j: for j>=1 use sigma(t_j)=1+(-1)^(j-1)beta rho^(j-1),
and for j=0 use 1-lambda<sigma(t_0)<0 and lambda<3.

Fix a sector matrix B_X of size N, and put C_j(X)=I-t_j B_X/4.
The quantity (4Q_j)^N det C_j(X) belongs to Z[sqrt(2)] and is nonzero
by Section5. Its product with its field conjugate is a nonzero integer,
whose absolute value is at least1. Thus

    |det C_j(X)| |det sigma(C_j(X))| >= (4Q_j)^(-2N).

The eigenvalues of B_X/4 lie in [0,1]. By (27), every singular value of
sigma(C_j(X)) is at most3; consequently

    |det C_j(X)| >= (48 Q_j^2)^(-N).                  (28)

Every singular value of C_j(X) is at most1+t_j. Divide its determinant
by an upper bound on the other N-1 singular values. With

    Cbar_j=48 Q_j^2(1+t_j)>1,

equation (28) gives the sector-independent estimate

    ||T_(t_j)(X)|| >= Cbar_j^(-N)||X||.               (29)

The constant in (29) is uniform in the actual active set at that step;
there is no need to commute successive Hessians. Multiply (29) along
ALL physical suffix steps with their exact multiplicities to obtain

    ||X_(ell_m)||
      >= [product_(j=0)^(m-2) Cbar_j^(2^(m-2-j))]^(-N)||v||.  (30)

For clarity, convert norm into the requested positive edge energy directly.
Let b=min X_i, e=max X_i. The inequalities
(X_i-b)(e-X_i)>=0, summed over a mean-zero vector X, give
||X||^2<=-Nbe<=N(e-b)^2/4. A directed path from a maximum to a minimum
and Cauchy--Schwarz, as in Section4, give
(e-b)^2<=M^2<=N||(DX)_+||^2. Therefore

    N||(DX)_+||^2 >= 4||X||^2/N.                    (31)

Using ||v||^2=N/2 in (30)--(31) proves the first explicit floor

    J_m>=2[product_(j=0)^(m-2)
                    Cbar_j^(2^(m-2-j))]^(-2N)>0.    (32)

This is an estimate of the actual terminal energy, not an intermediate peak.
It holds even if many large-step updates create negative mass.

To obtain the shorter bound (A), note a<1 and c>1, so
1+t_j=2+a c^j<=3c^j. The exact multiplicity sums are

    sum_(j=0)^(m-2) 2^(m-2-j)=2^(m-1)-1,
    sum_(j=0)^(m-2) j 2^(m-2-j)=2^(m-1)-m.

The second follows by subtracting twice the reversed geometric sum from
itself, or directly by induction on m. Equations (26) give

    sum_j 2^(m-2-j)log Q_j
      =2^(m-2)log(m+1)+(2^(m-1)-m)log m.

Hence

    log product_j Cbar_j^(2^(m-2-j))
      <=(2^(m-1)-1)log144 + 2^(m-1)log(m+1)
        +2(2^(m-1)-m)log m+(2^(m-1)-m)log c
      <=2^(m-1)log[144 c m^2(m+1)].

Insert this in (32) to prove exactly

    J_m>=2[144 c m^2(m+1)]^(-N2^m)>0.

The same proof also shows strict positive objective at every physical
query of the complete original word, since Section5 covers its central
level j=m-1 as well. This is a finite nonannihilation statement, not a
uniform positive asymptotic objective bound.

## 7. What this closes, and what it does not close, against the original benchmark

Retain the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark recurrences

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(s,t)=(s+t+sqrt((s+t)^2+4st))/2,
    G(s,t)=2s+t/2+sqrt(t^2+8st)/2,
    C_q=max_(0<=k<q)Omega(C_k,C_(q-k-1)),
    B_q=max_(0<=k<q)G(C_k,B_(q-k-1)), U_q^ZJ=1/B_q.

Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, its statement and proof Section7, gives
B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m->Z_1 with 0<Z_1<infinity. No conditional seed
assertion from that fact is used.

For completeness, the amplitude limit in the input draft can be justified
jointly as follows. The fixed rounding law gives

    d/lambda^m->exp(1)/lambda^2,
    N^2/lambda^m->pi^2 exp(1)/(6lambda^2), mu d->3/2.

Reindex r=m-1-j in (4). Then

    A_m=product_(r=1)^(m-1)
          [1-mu-(mu d-mu)c^(-r)]^(2^(r-1)).

Each subtracted quantity is in [0,2/3), by (3). For 0<=u<=2/3,
-log(1-u)=integral_0^u (1-s)^(-1)ds<=3u. Thus the logarithmic tails
are controlled by sums of 3*2^(r-1)[mu+(3/2)lambda^(-r)]. The total
mu contribution is at most3mu2^(m-1)->0. The remaining tail tends to
zero uniformly with the cutoff because 2/lambda<1. Each finite prefix
converges, proving

    A_m->A_infty=product_(r>=1)[1-(3/2)lambda^(-r)]^(2^(r-1))>0,
    b_m->A_infty/2.

Every limiting factor is positive, and the preceding summable bound
proves positivity of the infinite product. Together with (5), this yields
the actual identity and limit

    B_(2^m-1)V_m=K_m J_m,
    K_m=b_m^2 B_(2^m-1)/(2N^2)
       ->K_cycle=3lambda^2 A_infty^2 Z_1/[4pi^2 exp(1)]>0.   (33)

Let Lfloor_m denote the explicit positive right side of (A). It gives

    B_(2^m-1)V_m>=K_m Lfloor_m>0                      (34)

for each finite m. But

    log(2/Lfloor_m)=N2^m log[144 c m^2(m+1)]
      =[3pi sqrt(exp(1)/6)/lambda+o(1)]
                       (2sqrt(lambda))^m log m.      (35)

Therefore the right side of (34) tends to zero. This proves no positive
liminf for J_m or the benchmark ratio and no lower comparison near1.
In particular it does not approach the required threshold1/K_cycle.
It also does not show J_m itself tends to zero: a vanishing lower bound
is not an upper estimate. The full law could still succeed or fail.

## 8. Follow-through on the upper-bound lead: what mass and support omit

While this draft was being checked, guidance1b5f7ed2710ee51a supplied
preliminary finite observations from max4's separately authorized batch
and asked that a family upper bound be considered. I read the existing
public MAIN_PRELIMINARY_SUMMARY_READBACK.json and job04_m14.summary.json
in research/sparse_cycle_discovery_20261001. No numerical value or
observed sector pattern is a premise below; no trajectory or parameter
was evaluated by this worker. TASK's source-only update to the frozen
shared37ec6ea9a52bfbdf leaves the mathematical object and allowance unchanged.

One candidate upper route is to bound positive mass, then prove heat-type
spatial spreading over clock S_m of order N^2. For example, if one could
prove M_(ell_m)<=C and max_i r_(ell_m,i)<=C'/N with fixed constants,
then directly

    J_m=N sum_i r_(ell_m,i)^2 <= C C'.                (36)

This would already be useful if its constant were small enough compared
with1/K_cycle; there is no need first to prove J_m tends to zero.
Neither premise in (36) is asserted here. In particular, a long connected
active set, reflection symmetry, and bounded positive mass do NOT imply
the required bound on the largest coordinate.

Here is an exact same-cycle counterexample to that proposed implication.
Relabel edges so that reflection is i->-i. Choose an odd integer ell
with N-3<=ell<=N-1, and let A be the centered consecutive ell-edge
interval about edge0. Its complement is nonempty and is reflection
invariant. Let

    y_0=1,
    y_i=1/[2(ell-1)] for i in A other than0,
    y_i=-3/[2(N-ell)] for i outside A.

These are edge-coordinate labels, not time indices. The sum of y is zero,
its positive mass is3/2, its positive support is a single interval of
length at least N-3, and y is reflection invariant. Its energy satisfies

    N||y_+||^2>=N.                                   (37)

Every zero-sum edge vector is realized by a vertex vector on the SAME
cycle: solve x_(i+1)=x_i-y_i consecutively, use sum y=0 for consistency,
then subtract the mean. Thus (37) concerns genuine states of the same
static F. It tests a proposed uniform lemma, not the specified start -v.
It shows exactly the missing information in the mass/support upper route:
quantitative suppression of a localized positive peak.

The next attempted step was to get that suppression from one-step
positive-energy dissipation, followed by a clock-based smoothing bound.
That generic dissipation premise already fails at t_2 for a reflection
invariant legal test state. For N>6, put

    y=e_0-(e_3+e_(N-3))/2,

where the e_i in this paragraph are edge-coordinate unit vectors. This
has zero sum and is therefore D x for a mean-zero vertex vector as above.
Its only positive entry is1 at edge0; both neighboring entries are zero.
At an ACTUAL level2 step size, alpha=t_2/4, equation (6) gives positive
entries alpha at edges1 and N-1, a negative entry1-2alpha at edge0,
and the two unchanged remote negative entries. Thus

    M(y')=t_2/2>1=M(y),
    ||(y')_+||^2=t_2^2/8>9/8>1=||y_+||^2.            (38)

The inequality uses the exact t_2>3 in (8), not a numerical observation.
This is the first schedule level for which the argument can fail: for
t<=2, write y'=P y_+-y_-, where P=I-(t/4)L_e is nonnegative and
has every row and column sum1. Then (y')_+<=P y_+, and Jensen's
inequality at each row, followed by summation, proves
||(y')_+||^2<=||y_+||^2. The physical suffix reaches t_2 after its three
initial short steps. Example (38) does not assert that its test state
occurs at that actual time; it refutes the generic estimate that the
smoothing argument would need there.

To repair this upper route one must prove a property of the actual
incoming profiles at large steps, or a quantitative multi-step estimate
that charges the growth in (38) to preceding and following smoothing.
No such whole-block estimate has been derived here. Contiguous support,
reflection, and a mass cap cannot substitute for it, by (37).

## 9. Search, proof-attempt status, and precise handoff

The work followed plan51031bcdb5373455. The flux route was tested first
through a least-action barrier, then through the independent scalar
total-emission lower bound (20). The first route fails at its explicitly
identified legal-transfer assumption; the second fails quantitatively
even under the favorable extra mass cap, by (23). The separate spectral
separation route yields (A), whose limitation is quantified by (35).

Matlas searches for one-phase positive-part diffusion and for positivity
of Silver heat kernels returned unrelated porous-media, fractional-energy,
and variable-step BDF statements. A targeted robustness search did not
supply a theorem for the deformed schedule. A search on integer Laplacian
eigenvalues returned number-field arithmetic-matrix and covering-graph
results; none was needed or imported. Section5 provides the elementary
algebraic argument in full. The exact sandpile paper was read for its
proof mechanism; its missing assumption is checked in Section3. No new
external paper theorem is used as a mathematical premise.

No claim of a nonnegative full-block kernel, sign-persistence, uniform
mass nonincrease, continuum approximation, or order-independent clock
law has been established for this actual suffix. Those are not used in
(A). In particular the first three short steps do not justify any such
property at the later large steps.

The upper route added in response to guidance1b5f7ed2710ee51a is also
unclosed: (37) refutes the proposed mass/support implication and (38)
refutes its generic one-step dissipation repair. Neither is an assertion
about the actual terminal state's amplitude or a family obstruction.

Open interface: prove an actual-terminal lower law strong enough to
compare with1/K_cycle, or an actual family upper bound too small for
that comparison. This draft closes only finite annihilation and the
specified lower- and upper-transport proof attempts. No full target, new verified fact,
family impossibility, extra round, or permission for formal submission
is asserted. This ends the one authorized analytical round.

# Exposed affine-height segments: reduction, quantitative regimes, and reactivation

**JOINT UNVERIFIED.** Worker max4, project gd-exact-profile-20260926,
invocation86 after ended85, controller cap1. Steering: TASK.md and
master_guidance bb8c7f51e22c165f; plan b3ae4588b90dfc98.

No new fact is asserted. All new mathematical assertions below are parts of
this single joint derivation. No fact_submit, mathematical program, numerical
evaluation, trajectory replay, optimizer, consult, or subagent was used.
Administrative text operations and literature/memory retrieval were used.

The **complete exposed-segment quantitative profile remains OPEN**. This
report does not call a scalar recurrence or a list of cases a solution. It
closes several uniform regimes and gives a legal all-m obstruction to an
attempted simplification. Mechanism1 has not achieved its decisive closure,
so the gated triangle mechanism was not opened. The original target remains
OPEN and no complete minimax resolution is claimed.

## 1. Fixed parameters, accepted inputs, and scope

Fix any integer m>=1536. Throughout this report use the SAME parameters

    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, S=S_m, D=1+2S, n=2^m-1.

Here H_j and a,c are local schedule notation, as in the assignment; D is
the accepted fact's D_m. Every physical update of H_m, including its last
leaf 1+a, is retained. There is no child reset of m or eps.

Accepted fact d8b59c7d92425121 says that the original full finite-word
supremum W equals the supremum over finite-dimensional affine-height
polytope envelopes with the stated vertex bound, strict exposing height,
and nonzero initial point perpendicular to the height vector. Their
gradient is X-P_K(X-lambda), and their unique minimizer is0. We use that
fact only for this legality and representation scope. It does not reduce
the full supremum to segments and gives no quantitative rate.

Accepted fact 931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the benchmark comparison and its
conditional 799/800 reduction only after a full-class bound at17/(16D).
No converse to that implication is used here.

The length and mass identities needed below follow directly from the
literal concatenation. In particular

    eta=a/(c-2), 0<eta<1,
    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,
    d_j/A_j<c-2<1/2, and D<2*c^m.                       (1)

Indeed c>1+sqrt(2)>2 and c(c-2)>1>beta, so0<eta<1.
The displayed A_j starts at1 and satisfies A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j.
Since c^j>2^j for j>0, A_m<c^m. Also A_j>eta*c^j, proving
the bound on d_j/A_j, including j=0. For the numerical rational ranges
used only in symbolic comparisons below,

    12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1, 0<a<1/2.               (2)

For example sqrt(2)<17/12 and eps<1/1000 give
c<(29/12)(1001/1000)<5/2. These are uniform exact inequalities,
not computed horizon checks.

## 2. Scalar envelope, boundaries, gradient, and minimum

Let L>=0, K=[0,L]e_1 in R^2, lambda=(1,T) with T>0. Minimizing in
Y=(y,0) gives

    Phi(x,z)=phi_L(x)+z^2/2,
    phi_L(x)= min_(0<=y<=L) [y+(x-y)^2/2],
    y(x)=clip(x-1,0,L),
    g_L(x)=phi_L'(x)=x-y(x).                           (3)

Here L denotes normalized segment length, not the advertised smoothness
constant. All calculations use advertised smoothness1. Multiplying Phi
by any original smoothness constant L_orig>0 and using steps h_i/L_orig
leaves the trajectory and the normalized W=2R score unchanged.

The minimizer y(x) follows either from the derivative 1+y-x or from the
projection normal. Substitution gives the complete formula

    phi_L(x)=x^2/2,                  x<=1,
             x-1/2,                1<=x<=1+L,
             L+(x-L)^2/2,          x>=1+L.             (4)

At x=1 the adjacent values are1/2 and the adjacent derivatives are1.
At x=1+L the adjacent values areL+1/2 and the derivatives again are1.
For L=0 the two exterior formulas coincide with x^2/2 and the middle
interval is a point. Thus g_L is continuous and nondecreasing, with
successive slopes1,0,1. Integrating its slope on a partition at the two
breakpoints proves global1-Lipschitz continuity. Consequently phi_L is
convex, differentiable, and1-smooth. Its unique minimizer is0, its
minimum is0, and

    0<=phi_L(x)<=x^2/2                                (5)

because y=0 is always an available trial in (3).

The projection statement retains ALL normals: for every x, the selected
y(x) satisfies (x-1-y(x))(v-y(x))<=0 for every v in[0,L]. The vertical
component of X-lambda does not affect projection onto this horizontal
segment. Hence grad Phi=(g_L(x),z)=X-P_K(X-lambda) at every point,
including every original query, the terminal, and0. One fixed global
function is used throughout, so no separate row-feasibility oracle is
needed. The unique minimum of Phi is also0 at(0,0).

## 3. All exposed segments in all dimensions and exact orthogonality

Every nondegenerate segment allowed by the accepted representation has
the form K=[0,ell]e with ell>0 and e a unit vector. In fact0 cannot be an
interior point of a nondegenerate segment on which a linear functional is
strictly positive away from0: the two opposite directions would have
opposite heights. Thus0 is an endpoint. Write

    lambda=alpha*e+w, w perpendicular to e, alpha>0,
    X=alpha*(x*e+z), z perpendicular to e,
    L=ell/alpha, u=w/alpha.

Direct substitution into the defining minimum, with y=alpha*t, yields

    Phi(X)=alpha^2*(phi_L(x)+||z||^2/2).               (6)

The normalized coordinates obey exactly the original advertised
smoothness1 updates

    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_L(x_i),
    z_(i+1)=(1-h_i)*z_i.                             (7)

The factor alpha^2 cancels in the normalized objective/radius quotient.
Initial orthogonality is precisely

    x_0+<u,z_0>=0.                                   (8)

This accounts for all dimensions and all orientations, as follows.

* If u=0, (8) forces x_0=0. The horizontal coordinate stays0 and any
  admissible nonzero initialization is purely perpendicular quadratic.
  In dimension1 there is no such nonzero initialization.
* If u!=0, put T=||u||. All solutions of (8) with x_0=s are
  z_0=-(s/T^2)u+v, where v is perpendicular to u. Therefore
  ||z_0||^2=s^2/T^2+||v||^2. The canonical two-dimensional realization
  is v=0: lambda=(1,T), X_0=(s,-s/T).
* If s=0, horizontal motion is identically0. Every admissible nonzero
  z_0, including one perpendicular to u in higher dimension, has the
  pure quadratic score.
* For K={0}, the function is ||X||^2/2 regardless of lambda. Every
  admissible nonzero orthogonal initialization has that same score.
  If a particular ambient dimension and lambda admit no such point,
  they simply contribute no instance.

In particular a one-dimensional nondegenerate interval with scalar
orthogonality is not an admissible nonzero start. The extra direction
and its norm payment cannot be omitted.

## 4. The full-word quadratic factor and the exact weighted score

Define

    p_j=beta^(2^j-1)*c^(-j), p_0=1,
    Q=p_m^2.

For j>=1 the SIGNED product of all quadratic factors1-h in H_j is
-p_j. Indeed the length is odd, the leaf factor is-a, and
p_(j+1)=d_j*p_j^2, which is satisfied by the displayed formula.
Every nonempty prefix of H_j has product modulus at most a<1.
To prove this, the leaf case is a; a prefix lying in the first child
uses the induction hypothesis, and one passing its bridge has modulus
at most

    p_j*d_j = c^(-1)*beta^(2^j) <=a

times a prefix modulus at most1 of the last child. Empty prefixes have
modulus1. In particular this also proves the weaker all-prefix bound1.

Let x_n(L,s) be the actual scalar output of all H_m. For s!=0 put

    q(L,s)=2*phi_L(x_n(L,s))/s^2.

Equations (6)-(7), the true minimum0, and the full product give exactly

    score = [s^2*q(L,s)+||z_0||^2*Q]/[s^2+||z_0||^2]. (9)

This is a weighted average with the TRUE squared initial radius. The
canonical two-dimensional score is

    q_T(L,s)=[T^2*q(L,s)+Q]/[T^2+1].                 (10)

Extra dimensions only add quadratic weight. Equations (8)-(10), and
the s=0 cases above, show that the unrestricted exposed-segment supremum
is exactly the supremum of q(L,s) over L>=0 and real s!=0. One direction
uses the weighted average and the fact that L=0 already supplies Q;
the other uses finite T tending to infinity for each fixed scalar
instance. This equality is only for the segment subclass.

There is also an explicit FINITE tilt rule for a potential strict
witness. If q(L,s)=q_*>b, then

    T^2=1+2*(b-Q)_+/(q_*-b)                          (11)

gives q_T>b. This follows on multiplying by T^2+1 from
T^2(q_*-b)>b-Q. Also
|q_T-q_*|=|Q-q_*|/(T^2+1). A limiting scalar score alone is not
presented as a strict admissible witness.

## 5. Complete small-start and degenerate-plateau regimes

For every L>=0 and |s|<=1, all pure quadratic candidate iterates have
absolute value at most |s| by the prefix product bound. They therefore
remain in x<=1, where (3) has g_L(x)=x. This verifies the candidate
trajectory by induction through EVERY physical step. Its terminal is

    x_n=-p_m*s, q(L,s)=Q for 0<|s|<=1.               (12)

For L=0 this identity holds for every real s!=0. The zero scalar start
is handled by Section3 and does not require dividing by s.

Equation (1) also gives

    D*Q <2*c^(-m)<1/4, and p_m<1/(2*sqrt(D)),         (13)

because beta<1, c>2 and m>=1536>=3. Thus these complete regimes are
strictly below1/D, and (9) preserves this assertion in all dimensions.

## 6. Every no-crossing or one-crossing trajectory, all L and amplitudes

Here a strict crossing means consecutive nonzero iterates with opposite
signs; a zero hit remains0 thereafter. The argument is supplied jointly
and actually applies to any one-dimensional convex1-smooth function
with unique minimizing point0 and minimum value0. We apply it only to
the present family.

On either fixed ray let t be the radius and G(t) the positive gradient
magnitude. For a ray sign sigma in{-1,1}, write f_sigma(t)=f(sigma*t)
and G(t)=sigma*f'(sigma*t). Then G is nondecreasing,1-Lipschitz,
G(0)=0 and G(t)<=t.
If q=G(t), one has G(v)<=min(v,q) for0<=v<=t, whence

    2*f_sigma(t)<=2*t*q-q^2.                        (14)

For a segment of actual updates staying on that ray, with total physical
mass T, initial radius R and final radius t, radii decrease. All queried
gradient magnitudes are at least q=G(t), so R>=t+T*q. The identity

    (t+T*q)^2-(1+2T)*(2*t*q-q^2)
                         =(t-(T+1)*q)^2>=0

proves

    2*f(terminal)<=R^2/(1+2T).                     (15)

This includes intervals wholly in the outer quadratic region, intervals
that traverse either boundary, and arbitrary L. It is not a substitution
of a clipped-gradient formula for g_L.

Suppose there is exactly one strict crossing, at physical step h=1+d_k.
Let P,T be the COMPLETE masses before and after that step. If its query
has radius y and gradient magnitude v, the no-crossing prefix gives
|s|>=y+P*v>=(1+P)*v. Its crossing landing has radius
z=h*v-y<=d_k*v. Applying (15) to its actual no-crossing suffix yields

    q(L,s)<= d_k^2/[(1+P)^2*(1+2T)].                (16)

The chronological hierarchy pays this denominator at every possible
physical occurrence. Put A=A_k, d=d_k,
N=S_m-S_(k+1)>=0 and x=P-S_k. The full adjacent H_k children imply

    0<=x<=N, T=S_k+N-x,
    (1+P)^2*(1+2T)=(A+x)^2*(2A-1+2N-2x),
    D=4A+2d-1+2N.

The derivative of the denominator expression is
2(A+x)(A-1+2N-3x). It has no interior minimum. Its value at N minus
its value at0 is

    N*[2A(A-1)+N*(2A-1)]>=0.

Hence the denominator is at least A^2*(2A-1+2N). Since d/A<1/2,

    d^2*(4A+2d-1) <(A^2/4)*(5A-1)
                            <=A^2*(2A-1),

where the last inequality is equivalent to A>=1. Adding the2N terms
preserves strictness since d^2<A^2. Thus (16) is strictly less than1/D.
A trajectory with no strict crossing uses (15) on the whole word and
has score at most1/D. A zero hit gives zero scalar terminal value.

This closes ALL finite-L trajectories with at most one strict crossing,
without prescribing where either curvature boundary is visited. It does
not cover arbitrary repeated crossings: even the small-start quadratic
trajectories in (12) cross at every positive step, so crossing counts
must not be silently assumed small.

## 7. Complete forcing identity and uniform thin-segment bounds

For each physical query let y_i=clip(x_i-1,0,L), so0<=y_i<=L. The
actual recurrence can be unrolled algebraically, with no branch selected
or evaluated:

    x_n=-p_m*s+sum_(i<n) w_i*y_i,
    w_i=h_i*product_(i<t<n)(1-h_t).                  (17)

Let E_j be the sum of absolute values of these forcing coefficients for
the complete word H_j. Literal concatenation gives E_0=0 and

    E_(j+1)=(1+d_j*p_j)*E_j+(1+d_j)*p_j.

This recurrence is SOLVED here, not left as a quantitative answer. Put

    C_j=product_(0<=k<j)[1+c^(-1)*beta^(2^k)].        (18)

C_j in this report is the local segment forcing product, distinct from
the project's primitive benchmark capacities C_n. Its shared glossary
entry is C_seg_j.

Using p_(j+1)=d_j*p_j^2 shows

    E_(j+1)+p_(j+1)+1
               =(1+d_j*p_j)*(E_j+p_j+1),
    E_j=2*C_j-1-p_j.                                (19)

The signed sum telescopes to1- product(1-h)=1+p_j for j>=1.
Consequently the total POSITIVE coefficient mass in (17) is C_m,
and the total magnitude of its NEGATIVE coefficients is C_m-1-p_m.
Keeping these signs gives, for every actual scalar trajectory,

    -p_m*s-L*(C_m-1-p_m) <=x_n<= -p_m*s+L*C_m,
    |x_n|<=p_m*|s|+L*C_m.                            (20)

The y_i were not independently optimized to claim realizability: (20)
is only an upper bound on the actual coupled sequence.

The product (18) has the following completely evaluated uniform bound:

    C_m<6*sqrt(m).                                   (21)

To prove it take K=ceil(log_2(4m)). Since2^m>=4m for m>=4, K<=m.
Also2^K<8m. For k<K, each factor in (18) is at most1+1/c<sqrt(2),
so their product is less than sqrt(8m). For k=K+t,

    c^(-1)*beta^(2^k) <(1/2)*exp(-2^t),

using log(1-eps)<=-eps and2^K>=4m. The tail product is at most
exp((1/2)*sum_(t>=0)exp(-2^t)). Since2^t>=t+1, the sum is at most
1/(exp(1)-1)<1. Its exponential is therefore less than exp(1/2)<2.
These elementary exponential bounds follow for instance from the series
2<exp(1)<3<4. The full product is less than2*sqrt(8m)<6*sqrt(m).
If the tail is empty the same bound holds.

Combining (5), (12), and (20) proves the all-start bound

    sup_(s!=0) q(L,s) <= [p_m+L*C_m]^2.              (22)

For |s|>=1 use (20) divided by |s|; for |s|<1 use (12).
This is a quantitative bound for EVERY L, but it is not the desired
profile when L is large. In particular it closes the entire thin-segment
regime

    0<=L<=1/(12*sqrt(m*D)):
                 sup_(s!=0) q(L,s)<1/D.             (23)

Indeed (21) bounds L*C_m by1/(2sqrt(D)), and (13) gives the other
strict half. Equations (9) and (13) transfer (23) to all exposed-segment
dimensions with this normalized length.

For any L, the additional complete large-radius regime

    |s|>=1 and |s|>=2*L*C_m*sqrt(D)                 (24)

also has q(L,s)<1/D. One may replace the second threshold by the
explicit larger threshold12*L*sqrt(m*D). These conclusions allow L
and s to depend arbitrarily on the enclosing m.

## 8. Exact benchmark approach with a finite orthogonal tilt

The segment class is not uniformly below the scalar Huber benchmark.
For each enclosing m choose

    L=2S, s=D, T=sqrt(D),
    K=[0,2S]e_1, lambda=(1,sqrt(D)), X_0=(D,-sqrt(D)).

Every scalar query and the terminal lie in[1,1+L]. In fact at prefix
mass P_i the scalar coordinate is D-P_i, which lies in[S+1,D].
Thus the entire horizontal run is linear with gradient1,

    x_n=S+1, 2*phi_L(x_n)=D, q(L,s)=1/D.

Initial orthogonality is D-sqrt(D)*sqrt(D)=0 and the true initial
radius squared is D^2+D. The vertical terminal is p_m*sqrt(D), with
the sign obtained from z_0=-sqrt(D) and full product-p_m. Therefore

    2*Phi(X_n)/||X_0||^2=(1+Q)/(D+1)<1/D.            (25)

The relative deficit from1/D is (1-DQ)/(D+1), a positive number
less than1/(D+1). More generally increasing finite T in (10) makes
this score arbitrarily close to1/D. This is a legal lower approach
for the segment supremum, not a strict improvement over1/D or17/(16D).

## 9. Actual all-m upper-wing reactivation after a strict plateau query

This construction refutes the proposed shortcut:
"After an actual iterate enters the finite plateau, replace all future
gradients by the one-sided unbounded Huber gradient min(x,1)."
The static finite plateau can be left through its upper boundary again.
It does not refute the desired segment objective bound.

For every enclosing m>=1536 choose explicitly

    L=1/10, y=1+L/2=21/20,
    M=a*(1+beta)+a^2*beta*(1+d_2)
                      +a^3*beta*d_2*(1+beta),
    r=(y+L*M)/p_3, s=-r,
    T=1, lambda=(1,1), X_0=(-r,r).                  (26)

All quantities are finite and positive, and this is one legal segment
instance with initial squared radius2r^2. The scalar first seven steps
are the literal H_3 prefix of H_m. Its exact queried coordinates can be
specified in reverse order solely to prove the explicit forward law:

    x_7=y, x_6=-y/a,
    x_5=L+(L+y/a)/beta, x_4=-x_5/a,
    x_3=L+(L+x_5/a)/d_2, x_2=-x_3/a,
    x_1=L+(L+x_3/a)/beta, x_0=-x_1/a.                (27)

Substitution or multiplication of the three negative forcing
coefficients gives x_1/a=(y+L*M)/p_3=r, so (27) agrees exactly with
the completely explicit initial choice (26). Each negative query uses
gradient x. Every positive query among x_1,x_3,x_5 is STRICTLY above
1+L, hence uses gradient x-L:

* x_5>L+y/a>2, because y>1, a<1/2 and beta<1.
* a*d_2<5/4 and x_5>2 give
  x_3>L+x_5/(a*d_2)>L+8/5>L+1.
* x_3>1, a<1/2 and beta<1 give x_1>L+2>L+1.

These guards verify every update in (27), without a feasibility oracle.
The next actual query x_7=y lies STRICTLY inside[1,1+L]. Its step is
the original level3 bridge1+d_3, so

    x_8=L/2-d_3<0,
    x_9=a*(d_3-L/2)=beta^2*c-a/20.                 (28)

Here x_8 is followed by the original leaf1+a. From (2),
beta^2*c>2 and a/20<1/40. Therefore

    x_9>79/40>11/10=1+L.                           (29)

Thus x_9 is a genuine new upper-wing query after the strict plateau
query x_7. The physical next step at index9 is1+beta. Continuing from
the common state x_7, the proposed unbounded-Huber replacement agrees
at x_7 and x_8 but changes the next gradient at x_9. Its next iterate
differs from the actual one by

    x_10(actual)-x_10(replacement)
                   =-(1+beta)*(x_9-L-1)<0.          (30)

The original full H_m continues on the same globally defined function;
every remaining projection and its normal is fixed by (3). The vertical
coordinate follows its full quadratic product, and no radius is dropped.
This report does NOT supply an evaluated terminal payoff for (26), and
does NOT present it as an improving worst-case witness. Its concluded
claim is exactly the finite-prefix failure (29)-(30) inside a legal
full-word instance for every allowed m.

## 10. What has and has not been closed

The complete geometric reduction includes every dimension, all exposed
segments, zero horizontal starts, parallel height vectors, degenerate
segments, boundaries, the actual full quadratic factor, and the true
orthogonality/radius payment. The finite-T strict-witness conversion
(11) is explicit if a scalar witness is later found.

The quantitatively closed regimes are:

1. L=0 or0<|s|<=1: exact score Q.
2. Any L and amplitude with no or one strict sign crossing: at most1/D.
3. Every start for normalized L<=1/(12sqrt(mD)): strictly below1/D.
4. Every L and the large radii in (24): strictly below1/D.
5. The explicit plateau law in Section8: scalar score exactly1/D and
   a legal finite-tilt score (25) approaching it.

None of the following attempted simplifications closes the remaining
regime. Replacing all scalar motion by its quadratic product fails on
Section8. Replacing future motion after a plateau visit by an unbounded
Huber trajectory fails on Section9. Optimizing the bounded variables
y_i independently in (17) loses their common-body guards and yields
only (22), not a sharp family profile. These are failures of proposed
proof transfers, not evidence that the desired full upper bound is false.

Any as-yet-unexcluded scalar witness q(L,s)>1/D must in particular have

    L>1/(12sqrt(mD)),
    1<|s|<2L*C_m*sqrt(D),
    at least two strict sign crossings and no zero hit.

This is a necessary residual domain, not a claim of existence and not an
unevaluated branch list advertised as closure. Repeated returns with
reactivated positive outer curvature remain quantitatively uncontrolled
through all2^m-1 original updates. We have not established an all-segment
upper1/D or17/(16D), nor found a legal improving all-m terminal witness.
The triangle mechanism remains gated. Even a future segment or triangle
bound would not bound all polytopes in accepted d8b59c7d92425121.
